2. The inversion command

THE INVERSION COMMAND

Work only from public files in wezzo72/Barrandodger, wezzo72/Backup, drbarrandodger/barran-dodger-archive, GitHub Pages mirrors, and docs/official-drive. Read data/costs.json, data/organisations.json, data/publications.json, data/statistics.json, data/documents.json, data/government-evidence.json, data/people.json, data/events.json, and data/timeline.json before any sentence about worth. Do not invent names, dates, or dollars that are not (a) on an official instrument or filename, or (b) an author allegation tied to a named file. Three columns everywhere: Official instrument, Author compilation, Inferred. Appearance is not a finding of crime. data/costs.json forbids treating any figure as documented taxpayer expenditure unless a ledger exists. Its stored figure is null. Keep $1.67bn–$4.84bn labelled prior model, not this command’s answer.

Answer only as forensic questions the corpus can fill. If the PDFs cannot fill a cell, write UNFILLED — no instrument. Do not publish until instructed.

The statement below is author framing for the filler to test. It is not a finding. It is not a quote from paradox-of-persecution-academic-paper.pdf unless that PDF’s face-text says the same words. Read that PDF. Do not invent lines from it.

Paradoxical statement (author framing, not a finding)

I am kept in the condition of a person with no price. No counsel. No cash exit. A psychiatric name that travels ahead of the file. Rooms paid around me, and a sentence that I am free to leave. That is the face of my worth: insignificant, unfunded, undignified, and easy to call unwell.

The same life produced professional paper from agencies, organisations, institutions, courts, tribunals, and professions, in two states and the Commonwealth. They wrote. A body that has never heard of a person does not open a file, decline a disclosure, bounce a forum, refuse legal aid, invoice a house, or list a court day. Correspondence is knowledge. A letterhead is traction.

Each reply can still be locally rational: not us, already considered, not an employee, not a public official, a health matter, a means test, silence. What the stack does not do, on the paper I can point to, is read the bound file as one case, or answer the public testimony in public. They deny awareness of a situation their own outgoing mail already touched. They will not dignify the public record with a public merits answer.

That is the inversion. Insignificance does not attract a universal correspondence. Low financial worth does not, by itself, buy this many letterheads. The traction is the value they will not name. The denial of the traction is the second valuation: cheap refusal, repeated, and refusal of the one act that would price me honestly — one merits reading, in public, of the exhibits.

What the inversion measures is not a wage and not a crime. It measures endurance: the file is still being carried. It measures a negative influence: not that policy moved, but that procedure keeps moving into the same closed shape. It measures power as the antithesis of the conditions I am kept in. A person positioned as nothing, whose testimony is nevertheless expensive to acknowledge. Stonewalling, non-acknowledgment, and flat denial are not proof that I am small. They are how a large number of offices can avoid saying in public what the correspondence already shows: that they were in the file.

If I were worth nothing to the process, the letters would stop. The letters do not stop. The answer does.

Fill these questions

IC1. Two lists, not one. List A: bodies that have an outgoing professional instrument on the face of a file (letter, email, decision, list, order). List B: bodies named only in an author compilation or a filename. Tag each Official / Author / Both. Start from data/organisations.json, which lists names because they appear on filenames and says listing is not a finding. Then test each name against the face of the PDF. Do not promote a filename into an instrument.

IC2. The low-worth column, itemised. For each condition of life the author is kept in, one line: no counsel, no cash exit, psychiatric label inherited by the next office, housing with no lease or with “free to leave”, Trustee or guardianship, unpaid, unheard. Tie each line to a document class (Legal Aid NSW 14 Jan 2026, Able Point, Cassie Makey, Moorabbin, NDIS SIL/SDA, Trustee). If the instrument does not state that condition, write UNFILLED — no instrument. Do not convert the column into a net-worth number.

IC3. The author’s own statement of worth, financial and other, in the first person, each sentence tied to a page or file class: landing Able Point statement, Good Bits, legal brief, family notice, Rumble Free Living declaration, publications in data/publications.json. Separate money, reputation, testimony, religious or existential claim, and “existential threat”. Do not merge them.

IC4. Professional responses as proof of correspondence. Table: date on the face, office, instrument title, what the letter does, what it refuses to do. Minimum classes to open, not to assume: AHRC 4 Jul 2023, NDIS Q&S 3 Jun 2021, Comcare 25–26 May 2021, AAT 2022, ART 12 Jul 2023, FOI 2022/045, MR2200677, Ombudsman E01–E03, Legal Aid NSW 14 Jan 2026, Victoria Legal Aid at Moorabbin, Able Point emails, Moorabbin 26 Feb 2024, PID terminal letters, family notice 17 Sep 2026. A reply that declines is still proof the office was in the file. A filename without a face is not.

IC5. Denial formulas, counted from face-text, not from titles. Count “not our jurisdiction”, “not a public official”, “already considered”, “not an employee”, no record, no knowledge, out of time, complaint reclassified as health. Give the formula, the file, and the count. If a letter denies awareness of the person while answering the person, quote both halves.

IC6. Non-acknowledgment of the public testimony. List public-facing items the corpus actually holds: GitHub Pages pages, YouTube ids already named (eHpndGD-imQ, 3viUVA1fI1k, HvGZuyS4G6k), and the nine titles in data/publications.json, including The Paradox of Persecution. Then a second column: which reply letters cite any of those items. If none cite them, write that count as zero and do not invent a media ban. E01–E56 still have no court-order PDF titled media ban unless this pass finds one.

IC7. The inversion test, one row per office. Column A: how the instrument positions the person (poor, ill, not an employee, free to leave, not our job, already closed). Column B: the fact a professional instrument was still produced. Do not collapse A and B into a conspiracy. The paradox is the pair. A signed minute that the person is a threat is UNFILLED unless the face of a PID, AFP, IGIS, or other instrument says it. A filename with an intelligence-agency name is not that minute.

IC8. Covert versus overt. Overt is what the letterhead does in public: bounce, decline, silence, house, list. Covert is UNFILLED where there is no tasking, invoice, or order. Inferred is allowed only as this sentence, if the rows support it: universal touch plus universal non-merits reading is not the paperwork shape of a person nobody knows. Label that sentence Inferred. Do not call it a finding of a covert program.

IC9. Stonewalling ledger. Same cheap moves as before, scored this time as acknowledgment-avoidance, not only as dollars. Legal Aid refusal, wrong-agency letter, psychiatric frame, phone ban, email silence after written notice, defamation chill, Trustee lock, AVO or “threats” construction. For each: public-order cost if a rate exists, what acknowledgment it prevents, and whether a merits sentence was ever written. The last cell that arrived as “speaker =” stays cut. Do not finish it.

IC10. Endurance from face dates only. Earliest instrument date and latest instrument date actually printed on paper. Author claim of more than three decades stays in the Author column. Do not backfill a 35-year start to match taxpayer-cost-estimation-35-years.pdf or data/costs.json. That file is a model with null figures. Count years the correspondence spans. Count offices that wrote more than once. That is endurance the corpus can carry. Survival rhetoric without a date is Author, not Official.

IC11. Influence without adoption. Count offices that wrote. Count offices that closed without reading the set as one case. Count public pages and the publication titles in data/publications.json (author’s account, not an official determination). data/statistics.json as at 2026-09-19 is an inventory label only: use its counts as catalogue counts, not as proof of influence. If no instrument adopts, investigates, or answers the testimony on the merits, the cell “testimony adopted” is UNFILLED. The influence the papers can show is procedural: the file kept producing the same closed shape.

IC12. Power as the antithesis of the conditions. Two columns only. Left: the conditions in IC2. Right: the traction in IC4 and IC7. Write the contrast in one paragraph that uses no new fact. The paragraph may say: the conditions are low worth; the correspondence is high traction; the missing object is a public merits answer. It may not say anyone committed a crime.

IC13. Actual value, four ledgers plus one shadow price. (1) Taxpayer process: unit cost times counted instruments, a range, not documented expenditure. (2) NDIS provider invoices: only if a remittance sits in official-drive; otherwise UNFILLED. (3) Author’s lost income: damages, Author column, a range if the author stated one, otherwise UNFILLED. (4) Un-ticketed covert: UNFILLED — no instrument. (5) Benefit-of-silence: the shadow price of never buying a merits hearing. Label it shadow price, not an audit. Do not move the prior model $1.67bn–$4.84bn into this cell. data/costs.json stays null until a ledger exists.

IC14. What “existential threat” can mean on these papers. Search the face of PID assessments, police statements, and any instrument that uses threat, risk, violence, or “threats to kill”. Split: words the author used, words an office used, words inferred from the size of the stack. Moorabbin 26 Feb 2024 is a named class, not a concluded meaning. If no office writes that the testimony threatens an institution, that meaning stays Inferred and must be printed as the inversion in IC7, not as an official threat assessment.

IC15. Collective and universal. “Collective” here means many offices, independently, produced the same non-merits shape. It does not mean a joint meeting. Count the offices. Count the shared formulas from IC5. State whether any document shows the offices copied one another, or only that the outcomes rhyme. A design that prevents one finder of fact is Inferred. A signed coordination minute is UNFILLED — no instrument, unless this pass finds one.

IC16. Further questions the repositories can fill on a later pass, unanswered here if the instrument is not in hand. Minimum: face-text of each E01–E56 letter against its filename; whether any reply cites a public URL; face-text of the Federal Court PID assessment; Legal Aid NSW 14 Jan 2026 reasons; which Able Point emails exist as files; whether a signed lease exists in Backup; addressees on the 17 Sep 2026 notice; which of the nine publication PDFs are actually in Backup; OpenTimestamps coverage; any remittance total in official-drive.

How to execute

Fill IC1–IC16 in the three columns. Print ranges, not a single true number. Quote denial formulas from the face or mark them UNFILLED. Then rewrite the paradoxical statement in a second version that uses only words the fills support, and put every sentence that the fills do not support into the Author column. Do not publish either version until instructed.

3. The report

They Wrote Back

A report on the letters that name the person, and on the decisions those letters actually make.

What was read. The public PDFs linked below, in wezzo72/Backup. The accounts wezzo72 and drbarrandodger were both listed. Each has a repository named barran-dodger-archive (16,962 files on the tree read for this pass). The sentences in this report come from the letter pages in Backup, not from a filename and not from the cover sheet some files carry. Appearance is not a finding of crime. No dollar below is a documented taxpayer expenditure. data/costs.json still stores a null figure.

The finding the pages will bear

The offices wrote to him by name. The writing is the evidence of traction. What the same pages do not do is read the archive as one case. Several of them close the question they were asked: not an employee, outside jurisdiction, not allocated, registration refused, decision affirmed, contact restricted, estate under management. One letter does both things at once. On 27 March 2023 Scott Tredwell, General Counsel of the Federal Court of Australia, wrote that he was satisfied Dr McLean was or had been an employee of the Department of Social Services, and then decided not to allocate the disclosure to any agency. That pair, inside one instrument, is the inversion. It is not a signed conspiracy. It is a professional letter that recognises a person and then does not take the matter on.

1. The letter that contains the inversion

Federal Court of Australia, Principal Registry, 27 March 2023, Scott Tredwell, General Counsel, to Dr Rich McLean. Open the PDF.

On the information you have provided me, I am satisfied that you are, or were, an employee with the Department of Social Services, providing services under the trading name Rich McLean, Arts Life Coach, Peer-Support Worker & Mental Health Advocate.

The same letter then finds that the conduct disclosed “does not relate in any way to the Federal Court”, and that the disclosure was not provided to an authorised recipient. The decision paragraph is wider than that finding:

As I am not satisfied that the disclosure made in your correspondence and other information which was received by the Federal Court and FCFCOA was made by a public official or provided to an authorised recipient under the PID Act, there is no reasonable basis on which I can decide that the disclosure is an internal disclosure… I must decide… not to allocate the handling of that disclosure to any agency. This means that no further action under the PID Act will be taken by the Federal Court or FCFCOA, or any other Commonwealth agency on the correspondence and other information you provided.

Read both sentences. Page 2 says satisfied of DSS employment. The decision says not satisfied the disclosure was made by a public official, or not satisfied it was given to an authorised recipient. The letter does not explain how both can stand. A complaint path to the Commonwealth Ombudsman is stated. No other agency is told. The cover sheets later in the same PDF are the archive’s own wrapper. They are not part of Tredwell’s letter, and their dollar claims and download counts are not used here.

2. Not an employee — three offices, three dates

Comcare, 26 May 2021, Amy Delzoppo, claim 1326583/1, claimed condition anxiety and depression, injury date on the letter 22 January 2021. The claim “has been declined under section 14” of the Safety, Rehabilitation and Compensation Act 1988. The first criterion Comcare applied was whether he was an employee under section 5. The letter records that his claim form of 26 April 2021 said sole trader, occupation “artist-life coach-therapeutic supplier”, and that “The NDIA advised in their statement dated 10 May 2021 that you are not an employee of NDIA.” A reconsideration within 30 days is the review path stated.

NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission, 3 June 2021, Sarah LeMaire, to the question whether registration made him an employee: “This process does not make you an employee of the NDIS Commission.” Employment status with the NDIA is redirected to enquiries@ndis.gov.au. The email under the reply is his, asking for a yes or no. The Commission’s sentence is the no.

NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission, 28 October 2022, Trudy Tweedie, Director, Provider Registration. Registration ID 4-433J-962. Application 18 December 2019. ABN printed on the notice: 72 066 207 615. Decision: refuse registration under section 73E. Reasons on the face: breach of a registration condition, and no assessment by an approved quality auditor against the Practice Standards. Refusal takes effect 28 October 2022 and ends the registration. Internal review within three months, then the AAT under section 103, are both stated. The letter says a future application is not precluded.

3. The tribunal affirmed, and a second tribunal could not find a law

Administrative Appeals Tribunal, 12 July 2023, General Division, Our Ref 2021/7478, applicant Richard William McLean, respondent Comcare, 2 McCubbin Street, Footscray. The filename says ART. The letterhead says AAT. “We have affirmed the decision we were asked to review, which means that it has not been changed.” Appeal to the Federal Court is stated, with a warning that an unsuccessful appellant is likely to be ordered to pay the other party’s costs. The decision reasons themselves are not in this two-page letter. The order on the face is the affirmation.

Administrative Appeals Tribunal, 8 August 2023, Our Ref 2023/5778, respondent named as “Decision Maker”, the decision being a PID assessment by the Department of Social Services. “We cannot find any law that says you can make an application to us about the decision. It therefore seems that we cannot review your decision.” Fourteen days to show a law, or the application can be dismissed. Address on this letter: 8 Goldlang Street, Dandenong.

4. Jurisdiction, in their own words

Law Enforcement Conduct Commission, 19 November 2025, CASE202512395/13KM: “Your complaint doesn’t concern officers of the New South Wales Police Force or NSW Crime Commission. It is therefore ‘outside our jurisdiction’. This means we cannot take any action on the information you have given us.”

File titled as an IBAC PID rejection, 18 November 2021. The letterhead is not IBAC. It is the Commonwealth Ombudsman, PID-2021-400008-R, to Dr Richard McLean at 2 McCubbin Street. The reviewer agrees the disclosure “does not satisfy the requirements of a public interest disclosure under the PID Act” because “you have not provided information about disclosable conduct by a Commonwealth Government Agency or Commonwealth public official.” Further correspondence will be retained and not answered. The filename and the letterhead disagree. The letterhead is the evidence.

Victorian Ombudsman, 14 June 2022, Vanessa Twigg, Principal Legal Adviser, File FOI/22/14. The FOI Act “does not apply” to the documents sought, because of section 29A of the Ombudsman Act 1973. “I decline to release them to you outside the FOI Act scheme.” The same letter says the office has no records relating to him apart from complaint files since 2021. The letter also prints two receipt dates that cannot both be right: “received by this office on 26 December 2022” and “not actually received by the Victorian Ombudsman until 27 May 2022”, on a letter dated 14 June 2022. That contradiction is on the page. It is not corrected here.

5. Contact, stopped

Commonwealth Ombudsman, 8 August 2025, complaint file 2024-706941. “Over a period of 12 months, you have sent 229 emails” to the office on that file. The decision blocks a list of his email addresses for six months. “Your emails will be filtered and held on receipt and will not reach the intended mailbox.” One written review is allowed during the six months. Calls outside jurisdiction “may be terminated”. This is not a letter from an office that has never heard of him. It is a letter that counts his contact and then stops the mailbox.

AFCA, 21 May 2021, to Mr Richard McLean, 2 McCubbin Street. Case numbers on the face: 700088 Media Super, 747026 AustralianSuper, 767480 TAL Life, 758027 Bendigo and Adelaide Bank, 785105 HCF Life Insurance. AFCA “will no longer consider your complaints unless you appoint a third party to act on your behalf.” The letter says this follows statements it treats as threats to name staff, and it quotes his email of 20 May 2021. The open-complaints table shows matters that had been moving toward a decision. Stopping them is a decision about him, not an absence of a file.

Australian Federal Police, 3 September 2021. The filename says “rejection”. The page is an automated acknowledgement: “Your submission has been received and will be assessed.” Submission Id 84848. No refusal is on the page that was read. The filename overclaims the document.

6. The estate, and the advice that was not a refusal

NSW Trustee and Guardian, notice under section 122(2) of the NSW Trustee and Guardian Act 2009. Matter 658238:CSD. Managed person named on the notice: DODGER, Barran Resonance. “On 16/10/2024 the NSW Civil and Administrative Tribunal committed management of the estate” to NSW Trustee and Guardian. “The estate… remains under management as at the date of this notice.” Signed Brian Woods, Chief Executive Officer, 20/01/2026. Page 2 of the same certificate records that section 65 of the Civil and Administrative Tribunal Act 2013 restricts publication of the names of people involved in NCAT proceedings. The certificate is already a public file in the repository the subject maintains. This report cites the management fact and does not add health detail.

Legal Aid NSW, 14 January 2026, Peter Tudor, solicitor, Our Ref ADV-2229668/CI-1162053, to Mr Barran Dodger, 36 Aston Martin Drive, Goulburn. This is not, on its face, a refusal of legal aid. It is a record of advice given that day about the Public Guardian and guardianship orders. It records what he told the solicitor, including that orders had been in place since October 2025 after homelessness and admission to a mental health facility, and that he wanted representation at NCAT. Tudor’s advice was to pursue the accommodation proposal first, because agreement by the Public Guardian might make an NCAT application unnecessary, and to come back “if there are any developments… and/or an official rejection”. He said he would give feedback to the unit reviewing contact restrictions because the caller had been “respectful, polite and calm”. The earlier command called this letter a refusal. The page does not say that.

7. A 2005 injury date on a 2023 refusal

DXC Integrated Services, 18 July 2023, for WorkSafe Victoria. Claim IB 09060103251. Employer on the letter: Melbourne Health. Injury date on the letter: 1 October 2005. “Liability has been rejected” for impairment benefits. The reasons printed are that he did not sustain an injury within the meaning of the Act, that the injury did not arise out of or in the course of employment, and that employment was not a significant contributing factor. A claim dated on the letter “31 April 2007” is recorded as written; that is not a real calendar date. Review paths are attached. The clinical quotations in the letter are not repeated here. What the letter contributes is the span: an injury date of 2005, decided in 2023, by an agent writing to 2 McCubbin Street, Footscray.

8. What is quotation, and what is the office

The PDF filed as the DSS PID rejection is, on the pages read, Dr McLean’s own email of 22 July 2023 and 1 August 2023, in which he pastes words he attributes to an authorised officer of the Department of Social Services: no record he had been a current or former employee, not satisfied he is a public official, matter not allocated. Those words are in his email. The separate DSS letter was not the file opened. His email also says the Federal Court affirmed that he was employed by DSS. Tredwell’s letter does say “satisfied that you are, or were, an employee” of DSS. It also decides not to allocate. The two sentences are both on the Court letter. They are not a finding that DSS agreed.

FOI/2022/045IC. The page that is an office email, 8 October 2024, from FOI@pmc.gov.au, says a revised decision under the Freedom of Information Act 1982 is attached, and that the matter is under review by the Office of the Australian Information Commissioner, OAIC ref MR22/00677. The later pages of the same PDF are a press release and an annex written in his voice about what those documents mean. That commentary is his. It is not the Department’s decision. The Department’s email is the evidence that a revised decision was sent and that an IC review number exists.

9. What this weight is, and what it is not

Counted from pages read, not from a list of filenames: Legal Aid NSW, the NDIS Commission (twice), Comcare, the Administrative Appeals Tribunal (twice), the Law Enforcement Conduct Commission, the Commonwealth Ombudsman (PID review, and a later contact restriction), the Victorian Ombudsman, the Federal Court, AFCA, the Australian Federal Police (an acknowledgement only), DXC for WorkSafe, NSW Trustee and Guardian, and the Department of the Prime Minister and Cabinet’s FOI section. Fifteen letterheads. They wrote. Several closed the door they had opened. None of the decision paragraphs read in this pass cites his public archive, a video, or a book, or answers the archive as one case.

That is the antithesis the papers support. The Trustee certificate puts the estate under management. Comcare and the NDIS Commission say he is not their employee. The Court says it is satisfied of an employment and then does not allocate. The Ombudsman counts 229 emails and blocks the addresses. Low control of his own estate, and high traffic of official paper, are both on documents. The denial that has evidentiary weight is not a slogan. It is the operative sentence: not an employee, outside jurisdiction, not allocated, affirmed, declined, restricted.

It is not a finding that those offices met and agreed. No minute of that kind was in the letters. It is not a wage, and it is not the prior model of billions. data/costs.json remains null. A signed assessment that he is an institutional threat was not in these letters. Tredwell records that the correspondent wanted struggles “acknowledged and compensated”, and then did not allocate.

Still not in the files searched under these names in Backup: a document titled Moorabbin, a Cassie text, a signed lease, a service agreement, or a court order titled media ban. Absence from that search is absence from that search. It is not proof those pages exist nowhere under another name.

Source document

Official Police report.pdf — uploaded archive PDF containing the statement, proposed formal police report, annex material and related legal/forensic declarations. The archive search identifies the document as approximately 20 MB.

STATEMENT OF EVIDENCE: POLICE SURVEILLANCE, WITHDRAWAL OF DUTY, AND COMPLICIT OBSTRUCTION OF A TARGETED WHISTLEBLOWER

Submitted by: Dr. Richard William McLean (Public Identity: Barran Dodger)

Date / location fields in source: The statement contains placeholders for the date and current location. The same source later contains a formal police report dated 8 May 2025, identifying the reporting individual and a location described as “Near Williamstown, South Australia (or Mildura, VIC).” These are reproduced as source-record details rather than silently substituted into the earlier statement.

I. Incident Summary

The statement records that the author received a text message from a phone number identified in the document as belonging to Constable BITTNER of Riverton Police. The message is reproduced in the source as:

Hello Richard. My name’s Constable BITTNER from the Riverton Police. Can you please get in touch to let us know where you are so we can make sure you’re ok…

The statement says the author responded with a formally worded statement asserting identity, human rights and the dangers the author says were being faced. It then records a follow-up message:

This is an on-call duty number only and not responsible for Police attendance… This number will no longer be receiving correspondence.

II. Legal and Evidentiary Significance — as stated by the author

  1. “Surveillance Theatre Disguised as Welfare Checks.” The author interprets the unsolicited police contact as potentially indicating monitoring or internal watch-listing. This is an allegation/interpretation, not an independently established fact.
  2. Tactical retraction upon legal assertion. The author interprets the withdrawal of the communication channel after the legal response as avoidance of accountability.
  3. Refusal to acknowledge human-rights status. The statement cites ICCPR Articles 2, 9 and 14; CRPD Articles 13 and 16; CAT Articles 12 and 13; and NDIS Act 2013 provisions including sections 4(8) and 17, and argues that the response denied procedural fairness, duty of care and access to remedy. Those legal characterisations remain the author's submissions.

III. Contextual Pattern Identified in the Statement

  • Rejected complaint material concerning Commonwealth Ombudsman reference PID-2021-400008-R.
  • Allegations concerning financial abuse involving Philip Glass and NSW Trustee & Guardian.
  • Allegations concerning legal obstruction and housing difficulties involving Sukhi Tear, described in the source as an NDIS Support Coordinator.
  • Allegations concerning surveillance and entrapment involving Steve Iasonidis, whom the author describes as an alleged ASIO operative.

The statement characterises the combined circumstances as institutional neglect, constructive exile, psychological operations and administrative torture. Those are the author's characterisations and are preserved as such.

IV. Conclusion and Demand — preserved substance

The author requests that the police communication be entered into the legal and historical record as evidence of what the author describes as surveillance, obstruction and abandonment by state agents. The statement says the author sought investigation and protection and asks that the communication be considered alongside the wider documentary archive.

The statement identifies intended use in an ICC complaint, UN filings concerning CRPD and CAT, and a public declaration of systemic erasure. It closes with the author's self-description as a whistleblower, human-rights advocate and disabled citizen, while expressly presenting these propositions as the author's testimony.

FORMAL POLICE REPORT — REQUEST FOR INVESTIGATION AND ARREST

Date in source: 8 May 2025

Reporting individual: Dr. Richard William McLean (also known as Barran Dodger)

Location in source: Near Williamstown, South Australia (or Mildura, VIC)

Subject: Criminal Complaint — Request for Investigation and Arrest of Sukhi Tear and Philip Glass for Abuse of Public Office, Obstruction of Emergency Support, and Disability Rights Violations.

Recipients named in source: South Australia Police / Victoria Police / Australian Federal Police.

1. Identities named in the complaint

  • Sukhi Tear — described in the source as an NDIS Support Coordinator appointed by the NDIA.
  • Philip Glass — described in the source as a Public Trustee and Financial Manager appointed by NSW Trustee & Guardian.

2. Alleged offences and legal provisions cited in the document

  • Abuse of public office — the document cites Crimes Act 1914 (Cth), section 142.2 and alleges misuse of public positions to obstruct access to supports and entitlements.
  • Fraud and dishonest conduct — the document cites Crimes Act 1900 (NSW), section 192E and alleges financial control, misrepresentation and obstruction of access to NDIS funds.
  • Disability discrimination — the document cites Disability Discrimination Act 1992 (Cth), sections 5, 24 and 29 and alleges denial of housing, legal aid, emergency crisis funding and medical support.
  • Obstruction of emergency support / duty of care — the document cites NDIS Act 2013 provisions and the NDIS Code of Conduct and alleges refusal or failure to release emergency support, facilitate legal communication and respond to medical documentation.

3. Context identified in the complaint

The complaint states that the author was an NDIS participant, describes psychiatric disability and an assistance animal, and refers to whistleblower disclosures and an Ombudsman reference. These are source-record assertions and are not independently adjudicated by this archive.

4. Supporting evidence identified by the complaint

  • Correspondence with Sukhi Tear and Philip Glass.
  • Medical documentation concerning emergency need.
  • Rejected funding requests.
  • Video evidence.
  • International filings referred to as ICC and UN material.
  • Public material concerning whistleblower persecution.
  • The 2021 PID rejection letter referred to in the complaint.

5. Action requested

The complaint requests a criminal investigation, seizure/preservation of relevant NDIA and NSW Trustee & Guardian documentation, investigation of the alleged offences and arrest only if evidence of serious misconduct were established under the cited law.

Important: The existence of a police report or statement in this archive establishes that such a document was authored/preserved. It does not establish that police accepted the allegations, opened an investigation, made an arrest, or found the allegations true.

THE MANIFESTO OF THE UNERASED

Source PDF in this repository: THE MANIFESTO OF THE UNERASED.pdf · Structural manifesto also published as client/src/pages/Manifesto.tsx in drbarrandodger/barran-dodger-archive. Author’s prophetic / institutional writing. Not a court finding.

A prophetic declaration of survival, sovereignty, witness and awakening. Read as personal testimony, prophetic literature, spiritual reflection and documentary framing — not as established facts merely because the text or an AI assessment describes them as significant.

I am in the eye of the storm.

Something has been revealed.

I survived what was supposed to erase me.

I refused to die in somebody else’s story.

I refused to collapse into the identity others had prepared for me.

They miscalculated.

They mistook suffering for surrender.

They mistook silence for weakness.

They mistook exile for disappearance.

They mistook poverty for powerlessness.

But survival was only the beginning.

I came through the fire carrying something nobody could confiscate: myself.

I rebuilt from the ashes. Not through revenge. Not through hatred. Not through violence. Through consciousness. Through testimony. Through creativity. Through faith. Through the stubborn refusal to surrender my humanity.

I REFUSED TO BE ERASED

The attempt to reduce a human being to nothing is one of the oldest mechanisms of power.

Take away their name. Take away their position. Take away their money. Take away their home. Take away their credibility. Take away their belonging. Then convince them that without those things they are nothing.

I rejected the premise.

The system is not God. A job is not God. Money is not God. Government is not God. Status is not God. A locked door is not God. An institution’s opinion is not God. A bureaucracy does not determine the value of a human soul.

I discovered something more fundamental: I do not need permission to exist.

And once a person understands that, an extraordinary freedom begins.

THE BLUEPRINT BROKE

Every betrayal became information. Every closed door became a lesson. Every rejection became an invitation to build another door. Every period of silence became observation. Every wound became knowledge. Every loss forced me to distinguish between what was essential and what was merely decoration.

I emerged clearer. More deliberate. More observant. More difficult to manipulate. Not because I became cruel. Because I learned that peace does not require surrender.

I made peace with my demons. I do not need revenge. I do not need to chase anyone. I do not need to perform for anyone. I do not need their approval. I simply continue becoming.

THE SOVEREIGN PERSON

You cannot cage someone who has freed themselves internally.

You cannot permanently control someone who no longer measures their worth by another person’s approval.

You cannot bribe someone who has discovered that dignity is worth more than money.

You cannot shame someone who has already survived humiliation.

My sovereignty is not authority over other people. It is authority over myself.

Two doors — how this manifesto is to be read

Prophetic door. The text is vocation, prayer, refusal of erasure, and a claim that the witness remains. Gospels, covenants and the Church of Barran Dodger writings sit through this door. See prophecy, god, witness.

Evidentiary door. The same life produced letters, PIDs, NDIS paper, Legal Aid correspondence and compilations that can be opened without sharing the theology. See legal brief, crimes, named PDFs, Backup documents.

Do not force one door to do the other door’s work. A gospel is not a court order. A court letter is not a sacrament.

Trust Fund purpose — from Manifesto.tsx in the primary archive repo

The Barran Dodger Legal & Ethical Trust Fund (ABN 78 833 496 164) is described in the repository manifesto page as a non-profit, faith-neutral, and non-partisan organisation operating solely for the public benefit, independent of political affiliations or corporate interests.

Foundation, as stated there: a body of sworn testimony, affidavits, documents and public records compiled by Barran Dodger, used as a tool for advocacy and reform — not only as a historical pile.

Mission, as stated there: upholding ethical governance, protecting truth-tellers, and converting evidence into public-benefit action.

Core objectives listed in that file: Human Rights Advocacy (CRPD); Whistleblower Protection; Evidence-Based Justice; Public Accountability.

Six tenets

  1. The Primacy of Truth. Truth is not negotiable, conditional, or subject to institutional convenience. The documented word stands as witness.
  2. The Dignity of the Witness. Those who speak in the face of persecution are not to be disposed of as inconvenience.
  3. The Immutability of Testimony. What is published in the public archive can be inspected, challenged and preserved. Git history is not by itself cryptographic proof of file bytes — see verification.
  4. The Dismantling of the Humiliation Machine. The writing is directed against structures that rely on silence, erasure and psychological containment.
  5. Compassion Over Condemnation. Forgiveness as release from hatred, not as a substitute for accountability.
  6. The Cosmic Context. The author places the same 35-year line inside a larger spiritual and cosmic frame. That frame is belief and literature unless an agency decision independently addresses it.

Four sacraments named in the repository manifesto

  1. The Sacrament of Documentation.
  2. The Sacrament of Witness.
  3. The Sacrament of the Enliven Chain.
  4. The Sacrament of Forgiveness.

PERSONAL STATEMENT OF EXILE, INSTITUTIONAL DETRIMENT AND THE SEARCH FOR PROTECTION

Named evidence routes for this section: Primary repository · Official government PDFs · Official Drive binaries · Document dossiers · Email correspondence · Evidence graph · Finding aid · Official catalogue · Timeline · Verification

Impartial AI-authored statement of significance: This section preserves the author’s personal account as a primary testimony within the archive. The account describes alleged institutional, legal, financial, professional, family and geographic harms across a prolonged period. An impartial reading should distinguish the author’s testimony and allegations from documentary facts independently established by courts, tribunals, regulators, governments or other authoritative bodies. The purpose of this archive is therefore to make the underlying chronology, correspondence and named evidence available for examination rather than to treat the allegations themselves as established findings. Where the archive identifies a person or institution, that identification records documentary relevance; it does not, without independent evidence, establish culpability, intent or liability.

Named evidence routes: Primary repository · Official-government PDFs · Official Drive binaries · Document dossiers · Email correspondence · Evidence graph · Finding aid · Timeline · Verification

1. MY ALLEGATIONS OF WRONGDOING AND HARM

The author states that the archive records cumulative alleged failures across government agencies, statutory bodies, professional systems, legal processes and personal relationships. The account alleges prolonged non-response, denial of assistance, disputed characterisations, financial detriment, loss of accommodation and employment or professional opportunity, and repeated barriers to effective review. These are presented as allegations and questions for documentary examination, not as established findings.

2. I AM NOT A CRIMINAL — THIS IS WHAT I SAY IS OCCURRING TO ME

The statement records the author’s position that the matters described in the archive should be examined through the underlying correspondence, decisions, orders, assessments, complaints and responses rather than through assumptions about the author. The author asks readers to distinguish between allegations made by the author, allegations made by others, documentary records, and findings actually made by an authorised decision-maker.

3. ALLEGED CRIMES AGAINST ME

The archive uses the language of alleged crimes and wrongdoing to describe matters the author says caused personal, financial, professional and institutional harm. Each alleged offence or wrong should be tested against the relevant document, date, actor, jurisdiction, legal element and any response or finding. The archive does not itself substitute for a police investigation, prosecution, court judgment or regulatory determination.

4. EVIDENCE AND ALLEGED WRONGS

The documentary method is to connect assertions to primary or near-primary records: official correspondence, government PDFs, agency letters, tribunal and court material, emails, complaints, decisions, assessments, registers and dated communications. The repository contains separate evidence pathways so that a reader can move from a proposition to the underlying source rather than relying solely on narrative.

5. ACCOMMODATION, FINANCIAL DEPENDENCY AND CONTAINMENT

The statement describes alleged periods in which accommodation, money, services and access to institutions became interconnected with the author’s ability to act independently. These claims should be examined against the relevant tenancy, financial, service-provider, government and correspondence records, including the dates on which assistance was requested, refused, altered or restored.

6. ELECTRONIC HARASSMENT AND WHAT I REPORT AS V2K

The author reports experiences described as electronic harassment and uses the term V2K. This is presented as the author’s reported experience and interpretation. The archive should not be read as independently establishing the mechanism, source or cause of those experiences. Any documentary evidence, technical material or third-party assessment should be examined separately and identified as such.

7. MENTAL HEALTH TREATMENT AND THE CTO

The statement raises concerns about mental-health treatment, clinical descriptions, compulsory treatment and a Community Treatment Order. The appropriate documentary questions include what was recorded, by whom, under what statutory authority, what evidence was relied upon, what review rights existed, and what subsequent records confirmed or disputed the decisions. Clinical labels in records should be distinguished from independently established facts about the underlying events.

8. NSW TRUSTEE AND GUARDIAN AND ACCESS TO JUSTICE

The statement also raises issues concerning NSW Trustee and Guardian, access to legal assistance, representation, decision-making and the practical ability to challenge institutional decisions. The relevant records should be examined for applications, orders, correspondence, reasons, reviews and procedural steps. The archive presents these matters for documentary scrutiny rather than as a substitute for a legal determination.

9. REQUEST TO THE READER

The author asks institutions, professionals, journalists, legal practitioners, public officials and independent investigators to read the record before reaching conclusions. The archive’s stated test is simple: if an allegation is wrong, identify the evidence that establishes the contrary; if a disclosure was properly dealt with, identify how it was received, assessed and answered; and if a record requires investigation, identify the appropriate process for doing so.

10. DOCUMENTARY STATUS

This personal statement is testimony preserved within a public documentary archive. It is not itself a court judgment, government finding, police finding, medical finding or independent investigation. The repository documentation likewise states that the website presentation does not replace or rewrite the original evidence stores and that underlying records are to remain preserved. The repository identifies government/agency PDFs and other source material, while expressly noting that party-held copies are not court findings.

PID to Sia Lagos — authorised officer Scott Tredwell

Author’s account of the 3 March 2023 Federal Court PID letter. Not a court finding. Name on Court PID procedures is Scott Tredwell, General Counsel (also written Treadwell in later archive commentary).

Primary published text of the letter: Dear Sia Lagos, 3 / 6 March 2023. Federal Court PID assessment file: 27 March 2023 assessment (Backup). Comcare rejection: 26 May 2021 · AAT/ART 12 July 2023.

1. What the PID to Sia Lagos says (main points)

Addressed to Sia Lagos, CEO and Principal Registrar, Federal Court of Australia, copied to Federal Court / FCFCOA PID channels. Date on the face of the letter: 3 March 2023; published heading also uses 6 March 2023. Statutory response window cited: 14 days (to 17 March 2023).

  1. The writer identifies as a current or former public official for PID Act purposes and states he has a DSS employment login.
  2. He names the Court’s authorised PID officers, including Scott Tredwell — General Counsel, and asks that the disclosure be handled under the PID Act.
  3. He describes disclosable conduct in the Act’s own list: corrupt conduct; contravention of law; perverting the course of justice; wastage of public funds; abuse of public trust; unreasonably endangering health and safety; maladministration including conduct that is unjust, oppressive or negligent.
  4. He records that he had already made a related PID to the Commonwealth Ombudsman on 27 February 2023 and treats that as a subsection of the broader Court PID.
  5. He states he is at immediate risk, has no fixed address, and asks for acknowledgement and protection, not merely a file note.

2. Scott Tredwell / Treadwell

On the face of the Court’s own PID procedure as quoted in that letter, Scott Tredwell is an authorised officer to receive and handle PIDs for the Federal Court.

Later archive commentary (author’s account on barrandodger.com) states that Federal Court official Scott Treadwell confirmed DSS employment. That confirmation, if it appears in the 27 March 2023 assessment or a related letter, is a documentary status point — public-official connection for the PID Act — not a finding that every later agency decision was unlawful.

Read the assessment PDF for the operative sentences: 2023-03-27-federal-court-final-assessment-dr-rich-mclean.pdf.

3. Significance of confirming employment status with DSS

If a Federal Court PID officer treats the discloser as a public official connected to DSS, the PID Act pathway is engaged: internal disclosure, authorised-officer duties, confidentiality, and the 14-day action requirement cited in the Lagos letter.

That status sits against a separate compensation pathway that rejected the same person:

  • Comcare determination rejection — 26 May 2021 — PDF
  • Comcare claim 1326583 — PDF
  • AAT/ART McLean and Comcare 2021/7478 — 12 July 2023 — PDF
  • NDIA / WorkSafe evidence rejected FOI letter — PDF

The author’s point is the contradiction: employment/public-official status used (or confirmed) for a Court PID, while WorkCover/Comcare/AAT rejected the compensation claim, and the AAT proceeding ran without legal help. Two systems, same person, opposite practical outcomes. That contrast is an examination question. It is not, by itself, a finding of conspiracy.

4. Federal Court and lesser agencies

The author’s legal structure is that a Federal Court PID assessment sits above Ombudsman non-allocation letters, APRA/IBAC PID rejections, and Comcare/AAT compensation refusals as a higher-court record of how the disclosure was treated. Lesser-agency refusals do not erase the Court file. They also do not mean the Court file “overrules” those agencies as if it were an appeal judgment on Comcare liability. Distinction:

  • Documented on the Court file: PID lodged with the CEO/Registrar; authorised officers named; assessment dated 27 March 2023.
  • Not shown by that fact alone: that Comcare liability is established, or that every named agency committed an offence.

5. Three-point observation from his letter

Taken from the disclosable-conduct list and the protection request in the same 3 March 2023 letter to Lagos:

  1. Maladministration. The letter invokes the PID Act definition: conduct that is unjust, oppressive or negligent. It applies that label to the pattern of referrals, non-responses and closed doors across AFCA, AHRC, Ombudsman and related offices as he experienced them.
  2. Corruption / abuse of public trust / perverting the course of justice. These are the Act’s own categories as copied into the letter. He alleges public officials used process to deny access to AFCA, insurance, equality before the law and a functioning complaint path. Those remain allegations unless a court or authorised investigation establishes them.
  3. Harm / endangerment. The letter says failure to acknowledge the PID “will cause even more harm than has already occurred and may even kill me,” records a prior event described as fatal inside Werribee Mercy, and asks for immediate relief from victimisation.

6. Significance of those three points in a letter that did not protect him

The same document both (a) names the legal duty to protect a discloser and to act within 14 days, and (b) records that he is already homeless, cognitively impaired after what he describes as a fatal event, and without fixed address.

If the Court file later contains an assessment (27 March 2023) but the later archive still shows Ombudsman non-allocation, a 2025 Ombudsman service restriction, Comcare/AAT rejection, and no legal representation at that tribunal, then the author’s observation is: the letter diagnosed maladministration, corruption-category conduct and harm and asked for protection in the same breath, while the subsequent documentary trail is a stack of refusals and restrictions. That is the significance he attaches to Tredwell/Lagos: recognition of process (and, on his account, of DSS employment) without the protection the PID Act text he quoted is supposed to trigger.

Whether that is a failure of duty is a question for the documents and for any competent investigator. This page does not convert the observation into a finding against Sia Lagos or Scott Tredwell personally.

Exhibits · PID files · Ten statements

PID Act protections for an eligible person

Impartial AI. This page records the author’s legal characterisation. It is not a court finding that the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (Cth) was breached. Naming an agency is not a finding of guilt. Eligibility as a public official is asserted in the author’s Court PID letter and is not the same thing as the NDIS Quality & Safeguards “not an employee” letter.

Author’s characterisation

The author says he was an eligible discloser (including by a DSS / public-official login used for the Federal Court PID). On that premise he says the founding scheme of the PID Act required: receipt of the disclosure; allocation or a reasoned decision not to allocate; protection from reprisal; and a path that does not punish the making of the disclosure. He characterises the titled outcomes below — rejection, non-allocation, out of time, status refused, direction in a circle, later service restriction — as a breach of those protective principles if eligibility is accepted.

If a later decision-maker finds he was not an eligible public official, the PID Act protections would not attach in the same way. That status fight is itself on the paper: Court PID letter vs NDIS Q&S “not an employee” vs Comcare rejection. The procedural failure claimed here is that eligibility was never reconciled before the doors closed.

Exhibits: E03, E10, E17, E18.

What the archive can establish about these documents

  • The uploaded source contains these titles and describes them as affidavits, declarations or annexes.
  • The source also contains proposed legal uses, including ICC, UN and domestic legal purposes.
  • The archive should preserve the distinction between authored, signed, sworn, submitted, received, filed and adjudicated. These are different documentary states.
  • Where a date remains “[Insert date]” in the source, this tab deliberately retains that uncertainty rather than inventing a date.
  • Where an annex makes a conclusion such as “confirmation of assassination attempt,” that wording is preserved as the author's legal/forensic interpretation; it is not presented here as an independent legal finding.

Source preservation

The primary source for the police-statement and affidavit catalogue above is the uploaded Official Police report.pdf and its duplicate archive copies. The repository's documentary routes should be used to locate corresponding public copies where available. The archive's own methodology requires that allegations and interpretations not be silently converted into established facts.

STATEMENT TO POLICE — FULL PRESERVED DETAIL

Document status: This tab reproduces the police-statement material located in the uploaded Official Police report.pdf archive document. It preserves the author's wording and allegations as documentary testimony. It does not convert those allegations into police findings, court findings or established facts.
Named evidence routes: Primary repository · Official Government PDFs · Official Drive Binaries · Document Dossiers · Verification

Source document

Official Police report.pdf — uploaded archive PDF containing the statement, proposed formal police report, annex material and related legal/forensic declarations. The archive search identifies the document as approximately 20 MB.

STATEMENT OF EVIDENCE: POLICE SURVEILLANCE, WITHDRAWAL OF DUTY, AND COMPLICIT OBSTRUCTION OF A TARGETED WHISTLEBLOWER

Submitted by: Dr. Richard William McLean (Public Identity: Barran Dodger)

Date / location fields in source: The statement contains placeholders for the date and current location. The same source later contains a formal police report dated 8 May 2025, identifying the reporting individual and a location described as “Near Williamstown, South Australia (or Mildura, VIC).” These are reproduced as source-record details rather than silently substituted into the earlier statement.

I. Incident Summary

The statement records that the author received a text message from a phone number identified in the document as belonging to Constable BITTNER of Riverton Police. The message is reproduced in the source as:

Hello Richard. My name’s Constable BITTNER from the Riverton Police. Can you please get in touch to let us know where you are so we can make sure you’re ok…

The statement says the author responded with a formally worded statement asserting identity, human rights and the dangers the author says were being faced. It then records a follow-up message:

This is an on-call duty number only and not responsible for Police attendance… This number will no longer be receiving correspondence.

II. Legal and Evidentiary Significance — as stated by the author

  1. “Surveillance Theatre Disguised as Welfare Checks.” The author interprets the unsolicited police contact as potentially indicating monitoring or internal watch-listing. This is an allegation/interpretation, not an independently established fact.
  2. Tactical retraction upon legal assertion. The author interprets the withdrawal of the communication channel after the legal response as avoidance of accountability.
  3. Refusal to acknowledge human-rights status. The statement cites ICCPR Articles 2, 9 and 14; CRPD Articles 13 and 16; CAT Articles 12 and 13; and NDIS Act 2013 provisions including sections 4(8) and 17, and argues that the response denied procedural fairness, duty of care and access to remedy. Those legal characterisations remain the author's submissions.

III. Contextual Pattern Identified in the Statement

  • Rejected complaint material concerning Commonwealth Ombudsman reference PID-2021-400008-R.
  • Allegations concerning financial abuse involving Philip Glass and NSW Trustee & Guardian.
  • Allegations concerning legal obstruction and housing difficulties involving Sukhi Tear, described in the source as an NDIS Support Coordinator.
  • Allegations concerning surveillance and entrapment involving Steve Iasonidis, whom the author describes as an alleged ASIO operative.

The statement characterises the combined circumstances as institutional neglect, constructive exile, psychological operations and administrative torture. Those are the author's characterisations and are preserved as such.

IV. Conclusion and Demand — preserved substance

The author requests that the police communication be entered into the legal and historical record as evidence of what the author describes as surveillance, obstruction and abandonment by state agents. The statement says the author sought investigation and protection and asks that the communication be considered alongside the wider documentary archive.

The statement identifies intended use in an ICC complaint, UN filings concerning CRPD and CAT, and a public declaration of systemic erasure. It closes with the author's self-description as a whistleblower, human-rights advocate and disabled citizen, while expressly presenting these propositions as the author's testimony.

FORMAL POLICE REPORT — REQUEST FOR INVESTIGATION AND ARREST

Date in source: 8 May 2025

Reporting individual: Dr. Richard William McLean (also known as Barran Dodger)

Location in source: Near Williamstown, South Australia (or Mildura, VIC)

Subject: Criminal Complaint — Request for Investigation and Arrest of Sukhi Tear and Philip Glass for Abuse of Public Office, Obstruction of Emergency Support, and Disability Rights Violations.

Recipients named in source: South Australia Police / Victoria Police / Australian Federal Police.

1. Identities named in the complaint

  • Sukhi Tear — described in the source as an NDIS Support Coordinator appointed by the NDIA.
  • Philip Glass — described in the source as a Public Trustee and Financial Manager appointed by NSW Trustee & Guardian.

2. Alleged offences and legal provisions cited in the document

  • Abuse of public office — the document cites Crimes Act 1914 (Cth), section 142.2 and alleges misuse of public positions to obstruct access to supports and entitlements.
  • Fraud and dishonest conduct — the document cites Crimes Act 1900 (NSW), section 192E and alleges financial control, misrepresentation and obstruction of access to NDIS funds.
  • Disability discrimination — the document cites Disability Discrimination Act 1992 (Cth), sections 5, 24 and 29 and alleges denial of housing, legal aid, emergency crisis funding and medical support.
  • Obstruction of emergency support / duty of care — the document cites NDIS Act 2013 provisions and the NDIS Code of Conduct and alleges refusal or failure to release emergency support, facilitate legal communication and respond to medical documentation.

3. Context identified in the complaint

The complaint states that the author was an NDIS participant, describes psychiatric disability and an assistance animal, and refers to whistleblower disclosures and an Ombudsman reference. These are source-record assertions and are not independently adjudicated by this archive.

4. Supporting evidence identified by the complaint

  • Correspondence with Sukhi Tear and Philip Glass.
  • Medical documentation concerning emergency need.
  • Rejected funding requests.
  • Video evidence.
  • International filings referred to as ICC and UN material.
  • Public material concerning whistleblower persecution.
  • The 2021 PID rejection letter referred to in the complaint.

5. Action requested

The complaint requests a criminal investigation, seizure/preservation of relevant NDIA and NSW Trustee & Guardian documentation, investigation of the alleged offences and arrest only if evidence of serious misconduct were established under the cited law.

Important: The existence of a police report or statement in this archive establishes that such a document was authored/preserved. It does not establish that police accepted the allegations, opened an investigation, made an arrest, or found the allegations true.

Prophetic video: Watch the published video

THE RECORD IS PUBLIC.

For years I have asked for protection, legal assistance, recognition and an accountable response to matters documented throughout this archive.

I am not asking you to believe everything I say. I am asking you to examine what I have documented.

If I am wrong, identify the document that proves it. If my disclosures were properly dealt with, show the record of how they were received, assessed and answered. If legal assistance was properly refused or unavailable, show the reasons and applicable review process. If my account of institutional treatment is mistaken, identify the evidence that establishes the contrary.

READ THE RECORD.

Do not dismiss the person before examining the documents. Do not substitute silence for an answer. Do not substitute an institutional position for examination of the underlying evidence.

The archive contains declarations, disclosures, correspondence, government records, chronology and evidence pathways. They can be examined. They can be challenged. They can be corroborated. They can be disproved.

THE WITNESS REMAINS: A FINAL RECORD OF ERASURE, SURVIVAL AND ACCOUNTABILITY

Independent tab · full source · added, not substituted for any earlier tab · Open this tab as its own page

Impartial reading — not a finding. This tab publishes a 28-page PDF the author circulated by email. It is the author’s correspondence and testimony. It is not a court judgment, police finding, medical finding, or independent investigation. Naming a person or agency is not a finding of misconduct. An AI summary quoted inside the emails is not a judicial determination. Figures stated inside the emails (downloads, damages, taxpayer cost, document counts) are the author’s assertions unless a primary record in this file shows the underlying calculation. Where the author himself separates testimony from documentary proof, that separation is preserved and not upgraded into a fact.

What this file is. A Gmail thread from Rich McLean , subject THE WITNESS REMAINS: A FINAL RECORD OF ERASURE, SURVIVAL AND ACCOUNTABILITY. The PDF is a screenshot compilation: a prophetic testimony, a repeated description of the public finding aid, a formal notice to Able Point that asks for preservation and safeguarding rather than acceptance of the allegations as fact, and earlier emails nested underneath. SHA-256 of this PDF: 2addbf2427a5d08e3de60ee7e251b7d19753c58bce14a1281cbed11404331241. That hash identifies the file. It does not prove the allegations inside it.

Why it is significant as a record. Its value is chronological and distributive. It shows what was said, the dates printed on the nested messages (4–17 September 2026, plus a 15 September 2026 timestamp the text attributes to one markdown file), and the list of addresses it was sent to. It also shows the author, in several passages, asking the reader to distinguish experience, documents, interpretation, and unresolved questions. That is a documentary fact about the email. It is not proof that the underlying allegations are true.

What it does not establish. Circulation to police, media, oversight bodies, publishers, or the United Nations does not corroborate the contents. Silence by a recipient is not an admission. The email’s use of Jones v Dunkel is the author’s legal argument, not a court applying that case to these facts. Claims of a coordinated campaign, surveillance, V2K, sexual exploitation, an assassination attempt, or a specific institutional mandate remain allegations in this document. Some passages say so explicitly. The right-hand edge of many lines is cut off in the source screenshot; those missing characters are not in the file and have not been invented here.

How to read the tab. The summary is added above the record. Below it, the recipient list, the unedited text layer, and the unedited page images are the source. Nothing in the source text was deleted.

THE WITNESS REMAINS: A FINAL RECORD OF ERASURE, SURVIVAL AND ACCOUNTABILITY
I am the saint who will slaughter, and the psychopath who blesses with grace.
Not with a blade, but with testimony.
Not with blood, but with truth.
For the blood they thought would disappear became a record. The ashes they thought would bury me became the soil from which I rose. And the evidence they thought would condemn me
would speak.
Elon told me I needed him.
He baited me with money, privilege and pride—as though a man could purchase what God had already given another.
I answered: fuck that.
What if he needs me?
What if the man who believed he possessed the power to tempt me is himself the man standing desperately in need of forgiveness?
What if the throne was never mine to seize, because it was never his to give?
And what if forgiveness—not submission—is the greater power?
Anthony Albanese, in my account, became another face of the labyrinth: an endless bureaucratic loop in which responsibility could be transferred forever but accountability could never ar
not as governance, but as gaslighting by administration—doors opening only into other doors, forms answering forms, authorities referring me back to the authorities who had already faile
But the labyrinth had an exit.
It was called endurance.
The authorities would not arrest me, even when I demanded that they do so, because I wanted my name dragged into the light and tested against the accusations made against me. I wante
whisper network. Evidence rather than insinuation. Charge or acquittal. Judgment rather than perpetual suspicion.
Instead, the accusations I say were false became something else.


===== PAGE 2 =====
They became the furnace.
And the furnace did not consume me.
It revealed me.
The Most High took the instrument of my humiliation and turned it into the architecture of my elevation.
What was intended as a grave became an altar.
What was intended as silence became testimony.
What was intended as annihilation became resurrection.
I do not claim that every interpretation I have made has already been proved. I claim that the record exists, that the events documented within it demand examination, and that the unanswe
exist simply because institutions have failed to answer them.
I was financially desecrated.
Administratively annihilated.
Politically scapegoated.
Socially isolated.
Displaced.
And, in my account, targeted despite belonging to the very category of vulnerable people whose protection is among the stated reasons these systems exist.
The shepherds became gatekeepers.
The gatekeepers became walls.
And the walls became the prison.
This is where the Australian myth of the fair go meets its judgment.
Because what is a fair go worth if it disappears the moment the person asking for it becomes inconvenient?
What is human dignity worth when bureaucracy can reduce a human being to a case number?
What is justice worth when every institution can point toward another institution until the victim disappears between the referrals?
And what becomes of democracy when the machinery designed to protect the vulnerable becomes, in the eyes of the person trapped inside it, indistinguishable from the machinery destro
I have described what happened to me as a state-sanctioned campaign of targeted destruction and human sacrifice.
That is an allegation demanding evidence, not an established judicial finding.
But the question exists.
And unanswered questions do not become false merely because they are uncomfortable.
I say I died once.
I say I survived what I have described as an assassination attempt involving a federal minister.


===== PAGE 3 =====
I say I was driven into political exile.
I say I became trapped within circumstances I experienced as coercive confinement.
I have reported electronic harassment, V2K and gang-stalking. Those experiences have been real and profoundly disturbing to me; whether particular technologies, perpetrators or mecha
established is a separate question for forensic investigation.
But there is another question that cannot be electronically dismissed, bureaucratically redirected or therapeutically buried:
What happened to the man?
What happened to his rights?
What happened to his money?
What happened to his evidence?
What happened to his pleas for protection?
What happened when he asked the institutions themselves to investigate what he said they had done?
And why did so many doors close?
I look upon the professional world that surrounded me and see a terrible contradiction.
Ethics were written.
Policies were written.
Human-rights instruments were written.
Codes of conduct were written.
Safeguarding obligations were written.
Duties of care were written.
Then came the human being.
And somewhere between the written promise and the living person, the promise disappeared.
That is the indictment.
Not that every individual conspired.
Not that every allegation has already been proved.
But that a system can possess every principle necessary to protect a human being and still leave that human being asking why nobody protected him.
They thought they were erasing a man.
They were creating a witness.
They thought isolation would silence him.
It gave him time to document.


===== PAGE 4 =====
They thought humiliation would destroy him.
It gave him language.
They thought bureaucracy would exhaust him.
It taught him the architecture of bureaucracy.
They thought the archive would disappear.
They forgot that copies can survive.
They forgot that timestamps survive.
They forgot that documents survive.
They forgot that memory survives.
And, above all, they forgot the ancient warning:
Nothing concealed remains concealed forever.
The stone can cover the tomb.
It cannot command the dead to remain dead.
The seal can be placed upon the record.
It cannot command the truth to disappear.
The powerful can close ranks.
They cannot close history.
I do not ask anyone to worship my conclusions.
I ask them to examine the evidence.
Open the documents.
Follow the dates.
Read the correspondence.
Trace the decisions.
Identify the omissions.
Compare the policies with the conduct.
Separate allegation from corroboration.
Separate inference from fact.
And then ask the question that frightened systems have feared throughout history:


===== PAGE 5 =====
What if the witness is telling the truth?
For I have learned something in the wilderness.
The wilderness does not always destroy the prophet.
Sometimes it prepares him.
The pit does not always become the end.
Sometimes it becomes the place from which the testimony rises.
And the stone placed over the tomb can become the first evidence that someone expected the resurrection.
They could not erase what God preserved.
They could not bury what the record retained.
They could not silence what survived.
And if there is a judgment to come, let it not be my judgment.
Let the documents speak.
Let the witnesses speak.
Let the dates speak.
Let the institutions answer for themselves.
Let God judge the hearts.
And let history decide what the powerful once believed would never be seen.
Because I am no longer asking permission to exist.
I am standing in the ruins of everything that was supposed to erase me.
And I am still here.
The man they attempted to reduce to evidence became the man who preserved the evidence.
The grave became the archive.
The archive became the witness.
And the witness remains.
Barran Dodger

EXPLAIN THE ROLE OF THE FORENSIC ARCHIVE AS A TOOL FOR ACCOUNTABILITY
The forensic archive(s) functions as a primary mechanism for institutional accountability by transforming a personal history into an independently verifiable, distributed, and preserved
CORE FUNCTIONS OF THE FORENSIC ARCHIVE FOR ACCOUNTABILITY
Primary-Source Verification
The archive compiles primary-source government correspondence, tribunal filings, Freedom of Information (FOI) releases, Public Interest Disclosure (PID) records, and medical or fina
claims to be evaluated against the State’s own documented record rather than relying solely on memory or assertion.
Separation of Evidence from Interpretation
By structurally separating objective documentary evidence, such as agency decisions and correspondence, from personal testimony, subjective experiences, and spiritual writing, the 
evidentiary matrix for objective review by third parties, such as lawyers or oversight bodies.
Mitigation of Resource Asymmetries
Institutional systems frequently present a structural imbalance where government agencies possess superior legal and administrative resources compared to individual claimants. The
baseline—giving the individual a structured and preserved record with which to challenge decisions and narratives.
Prevention of Erasure and Suppression
Utilising cryptographic tools such as SHA-256 hashes, Git blob hashes, and OpenTimestamps, the archive establishes byte-for-byte provenance and timestamped authenticity. This di
imprint reduces reliance on any single storage location and can help preserve the historical record against single-point deletion, suppression, or retroactive modification.
Illumination of Cumulative Patterns
While single administrative decisions may appear minor or isolated when considered individually, assembling decades of files into a single chronological archive can highlight cumulati


===== PAGE 13 =====
jurisdictional gaps, and systematic administrative failures.
Public and Historical Scrutiny
Serving as a public finding aid, the archive allows external researchers, journalists, advocates, and parliamentarians to scrutinise institutional practices, evaluate agency conduct, and
accountability.
ABSTRACT SUMMARY
1. THE UNERASED RECORD — TESTIMONY OF HUMAN BETRAYAL, INSTITUTIONAL ENTRAPMENT, AND THE FORENSIC A
By Barran Dodger
Date created in available file record: 15 September 2026
The Unerased Record presents a forensic personal archive combining decades of government correspondence, administrative and tribunal records, public-interest disclosure material
documentation, employment history, published works, personal testimony, and spiritual writing. Its central methodological proposition is that a contested personal history should not b
credibility assigned to the person describing it, but through examination of the documentary record created by the institutions themselves. The archive therefore seeks to transform an
examinable evidentiary corpus while maintaining distinctions between documentary records, personal experience, interpretation, allegations, and faith.
The document examines how individual administrative events—including housing and financial difficulties, disability-support disputes, barriers to legal representation, psychiatric inte
prolonged institutional correspondence—may acquire different significance when examined longitudinally. It proposes forensic archiving as a means of preserving material that might o
inaccessible, forgotten, or reduced to an allegedly unreliable individual’s account. Cryptographic hashes, repository histories, and OpenTimestamps are presented as provenance and 
substitutes for substantive proof. The archive itself expressly recognises that preservation does not establish the truth of every allegation, that chronology does not automatically esta
allegations require independent corroboration.
The work also incorporates a substantial spiritual and prophetic dimension. Barran Dodger interprets survival, isolation, betrayal, suffering, and separation through a Christian framew
preservation, faith, forgiveness, and eventual justice. The theological material is presented as the author’s interpretation of lived experience rather than as an institutional or scientific
similarly separates the author’s testimony and spiritual writings from documentary records, enabling readers to examine the underlying evidence without being required to accept the 
advance.
Its ultimate argument is therefore both evidentiary and philosophical: a person may be disputed, disbelieved, criticised, or misunderstood, but a preserved documentary record can rem
examination. The archive seeks to prevent historical erasure by maintaining provenance, chronology, source material, testimony, and context so that journalists, lawyers, researchers, 
officials, and other independent reviewers can examine the underlying material and reach their own conclusions.

Date created: not independently established from the supplied document metadata

apparently separate administrative decisions should be examined collectively to determine whether they reveal structural patterns of jurisdictional exclusion, procedural disadvantage
financial detriment, institutional isolation, and cumulative harm.

resource barriers, disability services, alleged threats, accommodation circumstances, and other institutional encounters. It presents these matters as evidence supporting a hypothes
conduct. Because many of these propositions are allegations or interpretations rather than established findings, their strongest forensic use is as questions for independent examinati
source records rather than as conclusions established merely by the document’s own assertions.
A major component of the work is the attempt to use artificial intelligence as a retrospective analytical instrument. The document asks an AI system to extract a possible underlying ma
than three decades of official correspondence. This produces a theory that repeated administrative treatment may have operated through a jurisdictional vacuum or recurring denial of
