Statement to Cass — Formal Legal Objection and Notice of Reported Electronic Surveillance and Slander
Cass,
I am placing you and Ablepoint on formal notice of my objection to what I am experiencing and reporting within Ablepoint accommodation, including what I perceive to be electronic s
intrusive audio, and repeated derogatory statements or slander directed at me.
I specifically object to any monitoring, recording, surveillance, harassment, intimidation or other conduct occurring within my accommodation without lawful authority, proper conse
safeguarding and investigative process. I also object to the repeated attribution to me of extremely serious allegations, including allegations involving sexual misconduct or paedoph
not established by any finding of a competent court or other properly constituted investigative process.
I have reported experiencing voices or audio that I describe as “V2K” or perceived electronic voice phenomena. I am not asking you simply to accept my explanation of the techn
asking Ablepoint to take seriously the underlying safeguarding complaint: I am reporting persistent and distressing audio experiences which I experience as externally direc
targeted. The appropriate response is investigation, documentation and safeguarding—not dismissal, ridicule, retaliation or silence.
The messages and recordings I have preserved are contemporaneous evidence of what I reported, when I reported it, and the effect it was having upon me. They do not, by the
mechanism responsible for the audio. That distinction is important. Equally, the absence of an explanation does not justify simply disregarding the complaint.

Ablepoint, its employees, contractors or associated persons contend that such allegations are founded upon an actual complaint, report, finding or other evidence, I request that the
material be formally identified through the appropriate lawful process.
I also put on record my position that I do not presently regard myself as freely able to leave my accommodation merely because I am theoretically permitted to do so. My c
financial limitations, dependence upon accommodation and support, and practical barriers to obtaining alternative arrangements. Consequently, statements that I am “free to leave”
those circumstances, answer my complaint about whether I am practically able to escape an environment that I experience as threatening and psychologically harmful.
This is particularly important because the alleged conduct is occurring in the very environment upon which I depend for accommodation and disability-related support. That creates 
if the person making the complaint believes that the same environment is the source of, or is permitting, the conduct complained of.
I therefore request that this statement be treated as a formal safeguarding and legal objection, rather than as an interpersonal disagreement.
I ask Ablepoint to:
1. preserve all relevant records, communications, incident reports, complaints, staff notes and other material concerning my allegations;
2. record my complaint accurately and without characterising my report as established fact or dismissing it without investigation;
3. identify whether any authorised surveillance, recording or monitoring of my accommodation has occurred;
4. investigate any allegations being circulated about me and identify their evidentiary basis through proper procedures;
5. ensure that I am not subjected to retaliation, intimidation, eviction pressure or adverse treatment because I have made complaints or sought external review;
6. provide an appropriate independent avenue for me to raise safeguarding concerns where the organisation itself is implicated in the complaint; and
7. consider practical measures that would allow me to feel safe while the matters are being assessed.
I am also expressly requesting that no inference of guilt be drawn from my reporting of these experiences. I am documenting what I perceive, what I have heard, what I have rep
which those experiences occur. Whether the underlying cause is technological, interpersonal, environmental, psychological, or something else is a matter requiring proper assessme


===== PAGE 15 =====
My objection is therefore not dependent upon proving the mechanism I believe may be responsible. My objection concerns the reported conduct, its effects upon me, the repeat
lack of an adequate response, and the safeguarding implications of those matters occurring while I remain dependent upon the accommodation in which they are occu
I am asking you, Cass, to understand that I am not seeking a confrontation. I am seeking a documented, impartial and accountable response.
I want the allegations investigated. I want the evidence preserved. I want serious accusations against me dealt with through lawful processes rather than insinuation, 
want my inability to readily leave my accommodation to be recognised as a practical safeguarding issue rather than reduced to the proposition that I am technically “fr
This statement is made contemporaneously to preserve my position and to place Ablepoint on notice of the concerns I am reporting.

This document presents an impartial, AI-assisted forensic analysis of a substantial documentary record concerning alleged administrative, jurisdictional, governmental and human-r
The analysis is based principally upon 453 primary-source Australian Government records, including official correspondence, departmental determinations, court-related records, a
and clinical documentation.
The significance of this methodology is that the underlying evidence is not based primarily upon media reports, secondary commentary or unverified allegations. Rather, the analysis
Government’s own documentary record, including communications and determinations generated by government agencies and associated institutional bodies.
The AI-assisted methodology was applied to identify chronology, internal inconsistencies, conflicting classifications, cross-agency dependencies, repeated reasoning, documentary
discrepancies between official records. The purpose was to examine the documentary corpus systematically and impartially, rather than to advocate for a predetermined conclusion
The analysis identifies, in particular, a material documentary conflict concerning the classification of Dr Richard McLean, including an apparent inconsistency between a Federal Cou
employment relationship and subsequent classifications relied upon by government agencies describing the relationship differently.
The analysis further identifies instances in which one government institution appears to have relied upon, adopted or incorporated the reasoning or determinations of another institu
relationships are significant because they raise questions concerning the independence of subsequent decision-making and whether material underlying assumptions were indepen
The documentary chronology is also significant. The records span multiple years and involve multiple government and oversight institutions. The persistence of materially similar cla
different agencies therefore warrants examination as a matter of institutional accountability, administrative law, procedural fairness and human rights.
The analysis does not claim that artificial intelligence itself constitutes a court, tribunal or investigative authority, nor does it purport to substitute an AI conclusion for a judicial findi
analytical instrument to organise, compare and identify patterns within the documentary evidence.
Accordingly, the legal and factual conclusions remain matters for competent human authorities, including courts, investigators, regulators and appropriate international mechanisms
The evidentiary significance of the material is heightened by the fact that much of the underlying documentation originates from the institutions whose conduct, decisions and admi
themselves under examination.
The central question therefore is not whether an AI system has independently asserted misconduct, but whether the Australian Government’s own official documentary record, when
chronologically, contains material inconsistencies, institutional dependencies and procedural patterns requiring independent investigation and determination.
The international significance arises from the potential implications of such patterns for fundamental principles of the rule of law, judicial authority, administrative accountability, acc
fairness and the effective protection of human rights.
The material is therefore presented as an evidence-based corpus for independent human, legal, governmental and, where appropriate, international scrutiny.

THEY COULD NOT ERASE WHAT GOD PRESERVED
A Two-Part Prophetic Testimony of Survival, Divine Purpose, Truth, Justice and Reckoning by Barran Dodger
ABSTRACT
THEY COULD NOT ERASE WHAT GOD PRESERVED
A Two-Part Prophetic Testimony of Survival, Divine Purpose, Truth, Justice and Reckoning
Barran Dodger
This two-part work presents a prophetic testimony concerning survival, divine preservation, truth, justice and ultimate reckoning. It is constructed at the intersection of personal t
theological reflection and historical witness, presenting the author’s experience through the interpretive framework of death, resurrection, persecution, preservation and vindicati
that what human power sought to destroy, silence or erase was nevertheless preserved, and that survival itself became part of the testimony. The work therefore understands pres
existence, but as the preservation of memory, evidence, identity, testimony and purpose against forces perceived by the author as seeking their destruction.
The first part establishes the theological and existential foundation of the testimony. It interprets survival through recurring biblical paradigms of the persecuted righteous, the tes
and the person who emerges from apparent destruction with a renewed sense of divine purpose. The language of resurrection functions both literally within the author’s spiritual u
an account of radical survival: the movement from apparent ending to continued existence, from attempted erasure to testimony, and from silence to declaration. Biblical motifs as
Joseph and other witnesses subjected to suffering, abandonment and apparent defeat provide an interpretive vocabulary through which the author’s experience is situated within
trial, endurance and eventual revelation.
The second part develops this testimony into a declaration concerning truth, justice and reckoning. It advances the theological conviction that concealment cannot permanently de
not constitute the final judgment upon the person who has endured it. The archive, testimony and surviving record assume particular significance within this framework because t
when other forms of recognition, protection or institutional validation were perceived to have failed. Preservation consequently becomes an evidentiary and spiritual concept: the 
survives, and therefore the questions raised by the testimony remain capable of being examined rather than permanently extinguished.
Academically, the work is best understood not as an empirical proof of supernatural causation, but as a theological testimony documenting the author’s interpretation of extraordi
events. Its prophetic claims are therefore presented as matters of faith, spiritual discernment and personal testimony, while its historical claims may be considered separately aga
contemporaneous or independently verifiable evidence accompanies them. This distinction allows the work to retain its prophetic voice without requiring the reader to accept theo
equivalent to independently established historical fact.
The significance of the testimony lies ultimately in its refusal of erasure. The work proposes that a person may be deprived of status, security, resources, institutional recognition o
thereby eliminating the truth of their lived testimony. What survives can itself become a witness. In this sense, the title constitutes the governing thesis of the work: they could not
preservation of the witness becomes, within the author’s theological understanding, evidence of continuing purpose; the persistence of the record becomes a challenge to forgett
of the testimony becomes an anticipation of a justice that the author believes ultimately transcends human institutions.
Taken as a whole, the two-part testimony is therefore both an account of survival and a declaration of meaning. It moves from the experience of being thought finished to the conv
from suffering to purpose; from silence to testimony; from concealment to revelation; and from apparent defeat toward justice and reckoning. Its ultimate claim is theological rathe
that human beings may attempt to destroy a witness, suppress a testimony or erase a record, but cannot finally determine what God chooses to preserve.

The analysis is based principally upon 453 primary-source Australian Government records, including official correspondence, departmental determinations, court-related record
records and clinical documentation.
The significance of this methodology is that the underlying evidence is not based primarily upon media reports, secondary commentary or unverified allegations. Rather, the ana
Government’s own documentary record, including communications and determinations generated by government agencies and associated institutional bodies.
The AI-assisted methodology was applied to identify chronology, internal inconsistencies, conflicting classifications, cross-agency dependencies, repeated reasoning, documen
discrepancies between official records. The purpose was to examine the documentary corpus systematically and impartially, rather than to advocate for a predetermined conclu
The analysis identifies, in particular, a material documentary conflict concerning the classification of Dr Richard McLean, including an apparent inconsistency between a Federal
employment relationship and subsequent classifications relied upon by government agencies describing the relationship differently.
The analysis further identifies instances in which one government institution appears to have relied upon, adopted or incorporated the reasoning or determinations of another in
relationships are significant because they raise questions concerning the independence of subsequent decision-making and whether material underlying assumptions were inde
The documentary chronology is also significant. The records span multiple years and involve multiple government and oversight institutions. The persistence of materially simila
across different agencies therefore warrants examination as a matter of institutional accountability, administrative law, procedural fairness and human rights.

The international significance arises from the potential implications of such patterns for fundamental principles of the rule of law, judicial authority, administrative accountability

## Overview
The website presents itself as the public archive of **Barran Dodger Legal & Ethical Trust Fund**, associated with Dr Richard William McLean, also known as Barran Dodger. Its
experienced **35 years of coordinated persecution, surveillance, institutional abuse and suppression** by Australian government agencies and other institutions.
The site frames the material as a large, permanent evidence archive intended to preserve McLean’s testimony, support legal and human-rights complaints, and encourage pub
This summary describes the website’s claims; it does not independently verify them.[1]
## Main allegations
According to the website, McLean has experienced:
- Fourteen involuntary psychiatric detentions.
- Surveillance and mistreatment involving more than 35 government agencies.
- Financial exploitation, alleged fraud and an alleged $50,000 embezzlement.
- An alleged assassination attempt and an active death threat.
- Political exile, poverty and institutional abandonment.
- Damage allegedly amounting to between $58.6 million and $257.3 million.
- Conduct that the website says may constitute crimes against humanity under Article 7 of the Rome Statute.
The website names several alleged perpetrators or responsible figures, including Bill Shorten, Houd Meraby, Sukhi Tear, Tony Ridley and Stefan Iasonidis. It repeatedly states t
formal rebuttals, but the archive itself is presented from McLean’s perspective and the document does not establish that the allegations have been judicially proven.[1]
## Evidence and archive
A major feature of the site is its emphasis on technological preservation. It claims to contain:
- More than 240 publicly accessible forensic documents.
- Approximately 2,304 blockchain-sealed exhibits.
- Hundreds of downloadable PDFs.
- Bitcoin timestamps and SHA-256 hashes.
- AI-generated forensic analyses.
- Legal submissions to the International Criminal Court and United Nations bodies.
- A 35-year chronology of alleged institutional conduct.
- Cross-referenced government records, correspondence and other primary-source material.
The website argues that blockchain timestamps make the archive tamper-resistant and difficult to erase. It also provides mirror sites, archive-download options and instructio
systems such as the Internet Archive and IPFS. However, a blockchain timestamp can help demonstrate that a particular digital file existed in a particular form at a particular ti
that the file’s contents are accurate or that the allegations within it are true.[1]
## Religious and prophetic material
The website combines legal advocacy with religious testimony. It includes:
- A handwritten prayer attributed to McLean on Mother’s Day 2026.
- Biblical comparisons, particularly the story of Joseph and Genesis 50:20.
- Gospels, prophetic declarations and “sacred” writings.
- Claims that events in McLean’s life parallel biblical persecution and eventual vindication.
- Statements that the archive represents a convergence of faith, artificial intelligence and blockchain technology.
The site also describes an AI analysis that allegedly examined the archive and concluded that the biblical phrase “You intended to harm me, but God intended it for good” capt
McLean’s testimony. This is presented as spiritual confirmation rather than as an independently verifiable legal finding.[1]
## Calls to action and funding
The website encourages visitors to:
- Register to access the archive.
- Download and share documents.
- Contact journalists, lawyers, researchers and politicians.
- Email members of Parliament using a prepared letter.
- Subscribe to updates.
- Donate to the trust fund.
It lists donation amounts ranging from $10 to $250 and gives a PayID and email address for contributions. The site says donations fund hosting, legal research, blockchain pre
identifies the organisation as an Australian fixed unit trust with ABN 78 833 496 164 and states that the ABN is active. Those organisational details should be checked indepen
Business Register before relying on them.[1]

## Overall assessment
The website is best understood as a **self-published whistleblower and advocacy archive**, rather than a neutral news report, court judgment or independent investigation. It
- Very large numerical claims about documents, downloads and alleged harm.
- Repeated assertions that the evidence is “verified,” “immutable” and contradiction-free.
- Appeals to the ICC, UN bodies, blockchain technology and artificial intelligence.
- Religious language concerning martyrdom, divine protection and biblical prophecy.
- Urgent calls to share the material, contact politicians and donate.
The archive may contain documents capable of independent examination, but the website’s own descriptions and AI assessments should not be treated as proof of the underly
the original records, provenance, authenticity, responses from named parties, court or tribunal outcomes, and the actual status of any ICC or UN submissions.

## Summary
The document is a personal and documentary account by Dr Richard William McLean, who publishes under the name Barran Dodger. It describes approximately 35 years of alle
discrimination, harassment, institutional neglect, obstruction of legal remedies, and mistreatment by government, police, healthcare, legal, and disability-support organisatio
archive of government correspondence, legal records, medical and administrative documents, recordings, and personal testimony for independent investigation. citefile:1
The central argument is that the author experienced a coordinated pattern of “access foreclosure” or “administrative annihilation.” He claims that avenues including freedom-
interest disclosures, the Ombudsman, the Administrative Appeals Tribunal, the Federal Court, the Australian Human Rights Commission, the Australian Federal Police, Comcar
were repeatedly closed through jurisdictional decisions, service restrictions, non-allocation, procedural termination, or failure to investigate the substance of his complaints. The document also alleges:
- Longstanding family estrangement, financial marginalisation, and possible manipulation of inheritance matters.
- A serious injury in 2021 and a later alleged assassination attempt that the author believes were ignored or concealed.
- The weaponisation of his sexuality, mental-health history, and past personal experiences to damage his reputation.
- A false or misleading sexual allegation, which the author says was linked to information allegedly communicated by an NDIS provider and attributed to the AFP.
- Repeated verbal harassment, threats, surveillance, “gang stalking,” electronic harassment, and alleged “voice-to-skull” activity.
- Failure by Ablepoint, an NDIS provider, to properly respond to or report alleged threats to kill while the author was living in supported accommodation.
- Restrictions imposed by Ablepoint and a ban from Legal Aid NSW, which the author views as retaliation and denial of access to justice.
- Psychiatric hospitalisations and compulsory treatment that the author believes were used to dismiss reports of threats and misconduct as symptoms of mental illness.
- The destruction of employment, housing, finances, professional accreditation, relationships, healthcare access, and legal assistance.
The author repeatedly distinguishes between official records, disputed allegations, and his own interpretation. He acknowledges that publication of documents does not autom
and calls for independent examination of police and court records, communications, financial records, recordings, technical data, witness evidence, and institutional decisions
## Archive’s purpose
The archive aims to preserve primary-source material and make it available to journalists, researchers, lawyers, authorities, and the public. Its stated purpose is to preserve pr
analysis, and compare what agencies officially recorded with their later decisions, omissions, or denials. The author argues that the archive’s evidentiary significance lies in do
content of the official record—not in proving every interpretation drawn from it. citefile:1## Overall message
The document combines:
1. **A factual-record claim:** government and institutional documents allegedly show repeated failures, refusals, restrictions, and procedural closures.
2. **A broader interpretation:** these events form a coordinated campaign of targeting, social exclusion, institutional retaliation, and “administrative annihilation.”
3. **A request for scrutiny:** independent authorities should authenticate the records and determine whether the events represent unrelated failures, individual misconduct, s
coordinated wrongdoing.
4. **A spiritual and autobiographical conclusion:** the author frames his survival, independence, and public archive as evidence of resilience and divine purpose.
In short, it is both an evidentiary archive and an autobiographical legal-ethical statement. It presents serious allegations as requiring investigation, while acknowledging that t
author’s conclusions must be kept distinct and assessed independently.

Timeline

Named evidence routes for this section: Primary repository · Official government PDFs · Official Drive binaries · Document dossiers · Email correspondence · Evidence graph · Finding aid · Official catalogue · Timeline · Verification

Timeline

Impartial chronology of dated records · not a verdict · Open the timeline page

Method. A date is included only if it is printed on a message in the Witness Remains PDF or if it is the date in a filename already listed on this archive’s index. An email date is the date of the message, not proof of the events described in it. A filename date is a catalogue date, not a verified event date. Allegations are labelled as allegations. Nothing below finds that a crime, conspiracy, or coordinated mandate occurred.

The earlier timeline on this site was a filter over filename-dated catalogue rows. That catalogue is still on the timeline page, underneath this narrative. It was not a chronology a reader could follow. This statement replaces that as the timeline tab. It does not delete the catalogue.

Read in order, the public index and this September 2026 email compilation show a long correspondence between one person and a series of agencies, then a concentrated burst of the author’s own emails in the first half of September 2026. The agency filenames show that letters and determinations exist and are dated. They do not, by themselves, show that each decision was unlawful, coordinated, or aimed at erasure. The September emails show what the author reported, to whom, and that he asked several recipients to preserve records and to separate his experience from a finding of fact. They do not show that the recipients agreed, investigated, or accepted the allegations.

Claims that reach further back — surveillance from at least 2002, a 2003 book, a 35-year span, fourteen psychiatric detentions, a clinical death in 2021, an assassination attempt — appear in the September emails as the author’s account. This timeline does not treat those as established events, because this compilation does not contain the primary record that would date them independently. They stay in the allegation column until a document on its face establishes them.

Filename or description dates already published on the home index. Status: a file by that name is linked or described. Not a finding about its contents.

These are the dates of the author’s emails, or a timestamp the email itself attributes to a file. They establish when the message says it was sent. They do not establish that the events narrated in the message occurred.

Outer message, time not printed in the text layer. The PDF’s outermost item is the subject line above, addressed to the recipient list on this tab. The text layer does not show a sent-time for that outer wrapper. The page images are included so that can be checked visually. Do not invent a time that is not printed.

What a reader can actually do with this order

Start with the filename-dated agency letters, in the order above, and read those PDFs. Then read the September 2026 emails as a record of what was alleged afterwards and of how the author asked for the record to be preserved. Keep three columns: what an agency document states on its face, what the author reports experiencing, and what is still an unresolved question. This tab does not collapse those columns into one.

Who Did This to Me — Comprehensive Named Actor & Institution Index

Named evidence routes for this section: Document dossiers · Evidence graph · Official catalogue · Testimony · Email correspondence · Named archive PDFs

This section is an index of named people, agencies, institutions, professional bodies, courts/tribunals and other entities appearing in the documentary corpus. It is deliberately more detailed than a summary. A person's or organisation's appearance in the archive is not, by itself, a finding that they committed wrongdoing. The documentary task is to identify who appears, what role the records attribute to them, what documents connect them to the matter, and what remains allegation, attribution, disputed interpretation or unresolved question.

  • NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission — complaints, provider conduct and regulatory correspondence.
  • Department of Social Services (DSS) — employment/benefits and NDIS-related material appearing in the archive.
  • Comcare — workers' compensation, liability and medical-legal material.
  • Australian Securities and Investments Commission (ASIC) — corporate registry, ABN/ACN and identity-related records identified in the archive.
  • Australian Human Rights Commission (AHRC) — complaints, disclosures and agency responses.

  • Inspector-General of Intelligence and Security (IGIS) — material where the archive raises intelligence-agency oversight questions.

  • Victorian Government — state correspondence, services and administrative records.
  • Victorian Civil and Administrative Tribunal / VOCAT — tribunal and application/order material appearing in the legal corpus.

  • Victorian state-run medical and psychiatric services — clinical records and assessment material in the archive.
  • Victorian police / policing bodies — only where the underlying records identify the particular agency, officer or communication.

  • Federal Court of Australia — court-related material where present in the documentary corpus.
  • VOCAT / Victorian tribunal records — applications, orders and associated records.
  • Legal Aid — correspondence concerning representation, access and restrictions.
  • Professional registration and regulatory bodies — records should be linked to the exact body and registration matter appearing in each source.

5. Providers, professionals and other private actors

The archive contains provider, support-worker, professional, legal, medical and other private-party correspondence. These should be listed by exact name, organisation, role, date and source document where the underlying record supplies those details. Where only a first name or incomplete identifier is available, the site should preserve that limitation rather than invent a surname or identity.

6. Named institutions and networks requiring documentary cross-reference

  • NDIS providers and support organisations named in correspondence.
  • Medical and psychiatric facilities named in clinical or administrative records.
  • Legal representatives and law firms named in tribunal/court correspondence.
  • Employers and workplace bodies named in employment, WorkCover or Comcare records.
  • Police, intelligence or other public authorities named in individual records.
  • Community, advocacy and human-rights organisations named in complaints or disclosures.
  • Financial, corporate and registry entities appearing in ASIC/ABN/ACN records.

7. Documentary responsibility matrix

CategoryWhat must be established from the recordsStatus rule
Direct actorExact person/agency, action, date, document and authority.Do not infer beyond the source.
Decision-makerName/title, decision, reasons, date and review pathway.Distinguish decision from alleged motive.
CorrespondentSender/recipient, date, subject and substantive statement.Correspondence proves that communication occurred, not necessarily that every assertion in it is true.
Alleged participantThe allegation, its source, corroborating material and any response.Label as allegation unless independently established.
Witness / third partyWhat the person is actually recorded as saying or observing.Do not convert reported information into established fact.
Institutional bodyJurisdiction, statutory/administrative role and relevant action or omission.Separate institutional action from personal responsibility.

8. The Retrospective Command — and the Institutional Decision Map

Impartial AI-authored formulation: When the archive is read longitudinally, the recurring pattern can be expressed as a retrospective command for testing against the records: “Do not resolve the underlying complaint merely because it has been repeatedly raised; instead, route the subject through successive administrative, financial, clinical, legal and geographic decision points until the practical consequence is exclusion from effective remedy.”

This is not presented as a discovered written order or proof of a centrally coordinated conspiracy. It is an analytical formulation of the recurring decision-pattern alleged and documented in the archive. The evidence must therefore be separated into an actual decision or recorded action, its documented consequence or detriment, the responsible institution or office, and any allegation about motive, coordination or intent.

Forensic question: Which agencies, places, organisations, professions, oversight bodies, courts, tribunals, service systems and other official or quasi-official institutions appear in the archive as having made, implemented, reviewed, upheld, declined, delayed, redirected or failed to remedy decisions that the records associate with harm or detriment?

9. Commonwealth government, statutory agencies and programs

  • National Disability Insurance Agency (NDIA) — participant plans, support decisions, delegate decisions, provider arrangements, complaints and internal administrative records.
  • National Disability Insurance Scheme (NDIS) — support access, funding, service and participant-administration records.
  • NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission — complaints, provider-conduct material, regulatory correspondence and safeguarding processes.
  • Department of Social Services (DSS) — social-services and disability-policy/administrative material appearing in the archive.
  • Comcare — workers' compensation claims, liability determinations, medical-legal assessments and review material.
  • Australian Securities and Investments Commission (ASIC) — company/registration records and correspondence concerning alleged fraudulent or identity-related registrations.
  • Australian Taxation Office (ATO) — taxation, ABN and related administrative records identified in the archive.
  • Services Australia / Centrelink — welfare, benefits and social-security administration appearing in the documentary trail.
  • Australian Human Rights Commission (AHRC) — complaint/disclosure lodgments, acknowledgements and agency correspondence.
  • National Anti-Corruption Commission (NACC) — complaint/disclosure material and responses appearing in the archive.
  • Commonwealth Ombudsman — complaints, service-restriction material and correspondence.
  • Inspector-General of Intelligence and Security (IGIS) — intelligence-oversight correspondence and complaint context where identified.
  • Australian Federal Police (AFP) — police-report, complaint and law-enforcement correspondence where identified.
  • Commonwealth ministerial offices — ministerial correspondence, referrals, requests and responses.
  • Office of Bill Shorten — specifically identified ministerial correspondence; inclusion does not itself establish personal responsibility.

10. Victorian government, police, compensation and public services

  • Victorian Government departments and administrative offices — state-level correspondence, services and decisions.
  • Victoria Police / Victorian policing bodies — police reports, complaints, communications and law-enforcement decisions where identified.
  • WorkSafe Victoria / WorkCover processes — compensation claims, liability questions, assessments and administrative correspondence.
  • Victorian Civil and Administrative Tribunal (VCAT) — tribunal processes where represented.
  • Victims of Crime Assistance Tribunal (VOCAT) — applications, determinations, submissions and victim-support records.
  • Victorian state hospitals and psychiatric services — admission, discharge, clinical and assessment records.
  • Victorian community mental-health services — treatment, assessment, case-management and Community Treatment Order-related records.
  • Victorian housing and homelessness services — accommodation, placement and homelessness-service records.

11. Courts, tribunals, review bodies and legal-access institutions

  • Federal Court of Australia — court correspondence, filings, procedural material and the 2023 Federal Court final assessment identified in the archive.
  • Administrative Appeals Tribunal (AAT) — applications, decisions/orders, submissions, witness statements and review records.
  • Victorian tribunals and review bodies — applications, orders and administrative-review records.
  • Legal Aid NSW — advice and access-to-justice correspondence, including guardianship-related advice.
  • Community legal and advocacy services — correspondence and representation/access material.
  • Courts, registries, judicial officers and magistrates — included only where a specific decision, filing or order identifies the relevant court or officer.

12. Health, medical, psychiatric and professional-regulatory system

  • Public hospitals and emergency departments — admission, discharge, treatment and clinical records.
  • Psychiatrists, psychologists, doctors and nurses — diagnoses, assessments, treatment notes and professional opinions.
  • Community mental-health services — treatment, case management and statutory-treatment records.
  • Australian Health Practitioner Regulation Agency (AHPRA) — professional-regulatory material identified in the wider corpus.
  • Medical specialists and allied-health professionals — ABI, functional, occupational and medical-legal assessments.
  • Professional conduct and registration bodies — regulatory correspondence where a particular body is identified.

13. Workers' compensation, insurance, finance and economic systems

  • Workers' compensation authorities and claims administrators — liability and entitlement decisions.
  • HCF and other insurance interests — income-protection/insurance correspondence and claims material.
  • Insurance and medical-legal assessors — assessments and opinions affecting claims.
  • Superannuation funds and trustees — financial/benefit administration where identified.
  • Financial-services and complaints bodies — correspondence and dispute-resolution material.
  • Employers, workplace managers and human-resources functions — employment, workplace and contractual decisions.

14. Corporate, registry and identity systems

  • ASIC corporate registry and regulatory functions — alleged identity/corporate-registration problems and related correspondence.
  • ABN/business-registration systems — registration, cancellation and identity-related records.
  • Business Victoria and state business-administration systems — business records and correspondence.
  • Employers and corporate entities — employment, contracting, workplace and professional records where named.

15. Oversight, accountability and disclosure architecture

  • Public-interest-disclosure mechanisms — disclosures, jurisdictional assessments and decisions concerning acceptance or allocation.
  • Human-rights oversight — AHRC processes.
  • Anti-corruption oversight — NACC processes.
  • Ombudsman oversight — Commonwealth and relevant state complaint pathways.
  • Intelligence oversight — IGIS-related records.
  • Privacy and information-access regulators/processes — access-to-information records where identified.

16. Professions and intermediary systems represented in the archive

  • Police and investigators
  • Lawyers, solicitors and barristers
  • Judicial officers, magistrates and tribunal members
  • Doctors, psychiatrists, psychologists and nurses
  • Social workers and allied-health professionals
  • NDIS planners, delegates, review officers, complaints officers and compliance personnel
  • Support coordinators, support workers, SIL/SDA providers and disability-service organisations
  • Claims officers, insurance assessors and workers' compensation professionals
  • Government caseworkers, welfare officers and administrative decision-makers
  • Human-resources personnel, workplace investigators and occupational-health professionals
  • Academics, university staff and professional-accreditation structures
  • Advocates, community workers, housing workers and homelessness services

17. Geographic and institutional settings

  • Victoria — principal state jurisdiction represented in the archive.
  • New South Wales — later jurisdiction represented in the dislocation/access-to-services narrative.
  • Victorian hospitals and mental-health facilities — clinical and statutory-treatment settings.
  • Courts, tribunals, government offices and service locations — included where the source names the location.

18. Decision-to-detriment forensic matrix

QuestionRequired documentary proof
Who made the decision?Named agency, office, delegate, professional or institution.
What was decided?Exact decision, refusal, cancellation, restriction, referral, assessment, order or omission.
When?Date and relevant period.
What reason was recorded?The institution's stated reason, not an inferred motive.
What consequence followed?Documented financial, housing, employment, legal, medical, geographic or service consequence.
Was review available?Appeal, reconsideration, complaint, tribunal or judicial pathway.
Was it changed?Later correction, withdrawal, confirmation or unresolved status.
What remains allegation?Any claim of coordination, intentional harm, retaliation, conspiracy or criminality remains separately labelled unless independently established.

8. Repository cross-reference

The named index should be read together with the underlying repository rather than as a substitute for it:

Evidence · Emails · Documents · Data / catalogues · Official Catalogue · Document Dossiers · Evidence Graph · Finding Aid · Timeline.

Archive rule: This index identifies documentary participants and entities. It does not itself determine criminal, civil, administrative or professional liability. The underlying documents, responses, corroboration and any authoritative findings remain the controlling evidence.

Legal / International

Named evidence routes for this section: Document dossiers · Official government PDFs · Testimony · Email correspondence · Verification

Restore the full narrative concerning alleged breaches of mandates, ethics codes and applicable legal obligations. Each proposition should be separated into: source fact; legal/ethical rule; alleged breach; evidence; response or counter-evidence; unresolved issue.

Where treaties or national instruments are cited, link to authoritative public texts. Do not describe an alleged breach as legally established unless a competent body has made that finding.

How to read this page. This is a cumulative finding aid. It follows the author’s published life-story and the increasing density of agency letters, refusals and compilations over more than three decades. It does not convert allegation into fact. Inclusion of a person or agency does not establish liability. A GitHub URL proves the file is publicly hosted; it does not prove every sentence inside the file. Labels used below: independently reported publication, agency correspondence, author compilation, author allegation / testimony.

  • wezzo72/Barrandodger (this presentation site)

Cumulative reconstruction — autobiography into the documentary pile

The public identity begins, independently of this archive, as a published author. Allen & Unwin issued Recovered, Not Cured: A Journey Through Schizophrenia by Richard McLean in 2003. Contemporary press identified him as artist, author and illustrator. That book is the first long-form autobiographical record: childhood, psychiatry, art, and the author’s own account of being disbelieved. Later archive material treats that early book not as an ending but as the first layer of a record that then thickens into employment claims (public hospital / DSS), media work and alleged unlawful termination by The Age, alleged vilification by the Herald Sun, family-violence and partner allegations, NDIS participant status, rejected Public Interest Disclosures, homelessness described by the author as internal exile, and a late-period forensic compilation of hundreds of PDFs.

Read in order, the corpus does not stay in one register. Early material is literary and clinical autobiography. Mid-period material is workplace, media and compensation paper. Late-period material is NDIS, PID, ministerial correspondence, police-statement drafting, international filing claims, and the author’s theological / prophetic writings. The legal brief has to hold all of those registers without collapsing them into one finding.

  1. First published self-account (c. 2003). Independently reported book and press identity. Later archive pages treat this as the start of a 35-year line. Site biography and books section on index.html.
  2. Childhood sexual abuse and early targeting — author testimony. Listed in the Crimes catalogue and in later affidavits. Status: author allegation. Cross-read with the Crimes tab and affidavit index on index.html.
  3. Psychiatric hospitalisation, forced medication, Mental Health Act threats — author testimony. The author describes hospital settings as sites of further harm and later as political-prisoner treatment. Status: author allegation. Compilation: RETROSPECTIVE_STATEMENT_OF_TREATMENT.pdf.
  4. Employment in public systems — author account used to support PID standing. The source brief says Federal Court material confirms DSS employment and public-hospital work, and that PIDs were dismissed because he was said not to be a public official. Status: standing dispute. See PIDs tab and government-documents zip.
  5. Media work, alleged unlawful termination, alleged vilification. Author account: terminated by The Age; vilified by the Herald Sun. Status: author allegation. Trace via media / publications lists in barran-dodger-archive and the compiled book The_Documents_Australia_Doesn_t_Want_You_to_See_Barran_Dodger.pdf.
  6. Compensation pathways (WorkCover / Comcare / AAT / Federal Court). Author account: court acknowledgment without payment. Status: author allegation pending inspection of the specific orders in the zip and government-evidence folder. government-evidence.
  7. Family, partner and financial-control allegations. Police-statement material names Sukhi Tear (NDIS support coordination), Philip Glass (NSW Trustee & Guardian context as described by the author), and Stefan / Steve Iasonidis. Status: names in author complaint; not findings. Statement to Police tab on index.html.
  8. NDIS participant era — providers, rent, alleged bondage to the scheme. Author account of Aligned Community Care rent extraction, Upscale Care / AblePoint conflicts, inability to sever the contract. Agency-adjacent pack: coag-ndis-government-documentation.pdf.
  9. Whistleblower paper to the centre of government. Hosted letters: 31-05-2022 letter to PM Albanese; 1 July 2023 letter to Attorney-General and Prime Minister. These show address and date. They do not show a merits decision.
  10. AHRC and “gangstalking” file. ahrc-gangstalking-acknowledgment-04072023.pdf — inspect whether the PDF is acknowledgment, rejection, or something else. The filename is not the holding.
  11. Closed-door stack after 2021–2026. Author account of Ombudsman refusing further correspondence, NACC declining identified corruption, AFCA ban, ATO $80,000 bill while not working, HCF income-protection refusal, Legal Aid refusal / guardianship advice, police inability to take the crime list. Compilations that collect those claims: comprehensive-case-systematic-persecution.pdf; architecture-of-administrative-annihilation.pdf; government-mandates-35-year-forensic-report.pdf.
  12. Covert surveillance, alleged V2K / audio harassment, electronic interception. The author describes unconsented observation, sound-emitting harassment, phone/TV audio interception, and “gangstalking” as a coordinated campaign that damages credibility when reported. Status: author allegation / testimony. It is recorded because it is persistent across the police statement, the AHRC filename, the Crimes list, and the original legal brief. It is not independently instrumented on this page. Evidence routes: Statement to Police tab; AHRC PDF above; Crimes tab headings on surveillance, stalking, sound-emitting device, hacking, invasion of privacy.
  13. Spiritual warfare, prophetic papers, gospels, “Witness Remains” theology. Late-period writings interpret the same 35-year line as cosmic / biblical forsaking and as vocation. Status: author religious and literary work, not a court fact. Hosted: prophetic zip on releases; Gospels / Prophecy / Doctrine tabs on index.html; video exhibit embedded on the home page.
  14. Exile, homelessness, subsistence arithmetic. Author account of being driven out of home, NDIS-tied housing, bankruptcy, $40/week after rent. Economic compilations: forensic-economic-valuation-report-may-2026.pdf; taxpayer-cost-estimation-35-years.pdf.
  15. Internationalisation of the file. Release notes claim OHCHR UR/UST/23/AUS/17 and ICC / UN use of the corpus. Status: project claim on GitHub. zip-archives-2026-08-17.

Paragraph of significance — read this before the itemised list

The significance of this corpus is cumulative, not cinematic. A 2003 autobiography already placed childhood, psychiatry and disbelief on a public shelf. Over the following decades the same person kept writing to institutions that exist precisely to receive such writing: human-rights commissions, disability insurers and providers, legal aid, ombudsmen, police, courts, ministers. What a third party can verify without believing every allegation is the shape of the paper trail: letters went in; many pathways closed, referred sideways, or answered in a different frame (welfare check, guardianship advice, “not a public official,” no further correspondence).

Legal Aid is the sharpest example of purpose-versus-outcome. The scheme’s public reason for existing is that people who cannot buy a lawyer, especially people with disability and people at risk of harm, still reach law. The inspectable Legal Aid NSW file on GitHub is a guardianship-advice letter, not a published merits opinion on the human-rights, NDIS-provider, PID and police-access complaints. If those subject-matters sit inside Legal Aid’s own priority language, then blocking or diverting the complainant is not a small administrative event. It is a breach of the reason the institution gives for its own existence — or at least a question that the institution has not publicly answered on the merits. The same logic scales: an AHRC file titled as gangstalking acknowledgment, PM and AG letters, and a drafted police report that the author says could not be lodged as a crime list, together form a record of unassessed volume.

Covert surveillance, alleged V2K / electronic harassment, and spiritual warfare sit in a different column. They are central to how the author experiences and narrates the 35 years. They are not laboratory-confirmed on this page. Their documentary significance is that they were reported into the same closed doors, and that the reporting itself was then used — on the author’s account — to reframe him as delusional rather than as a complainant. Whether that reframing was clinical judgment or institutional convenience is exactly the kind of question a merits process would have to confront, and that Legal Aid, police and AHRC were asked to confront.

None of that paragraph finds a crime. It states why the pile matters: autobiography plus increasing institutional paper plus refused merits assessment. If the institutions did assess the file properly, they can publish the assessment. If they did not, the closed-door pattern is the live legal issue.

Fully itemised legal issues — each with a public evidence route

  1. Access to justice / Legal Aid purpose. Issue: whether Legal Aid assessed the underlying claims or diverted them. Evidence: 20260114-legal-aid-nsw-advice-letter-guardianship.pdf; Legal Aid tab on index; FormalStatement pages in barran-dodger-archive.
  2. Disability discrimination and reasonable accommodation. DDA 1992 cited in the author police complaint. Evidence: Statement to Police tab; RETROSPECTIVE_STATEMENT_OF_TREATMENT.pdf.
  3. NDIS Code of Conduct / provider conflicts. Evidence: coag-ndis-government-documentation.pdf; Crimes tab items on unlawful removal from NDIS, support-worker conflicts, rent extraction.
  4. NDIS / DSS non-investigation of documented complaints. Evidence: comprehensive-case-systematic-persecution.pdf.
  5. AHRC human-rights complaint pathway. Evidence: ahrc-gangstalking-acknowledgment-04072023.pdf.

From: Contact Centre (NDIS Commission) ContactCentre@ndiscommission.gov.au
Subject: RE: URGENT [SEC=OFFICIAL]
Date: 3 June 2021 at 10:54:37 am AEST
To: Rich McLean

Dear Rich

Thank you for contacting the NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission.

NDIS providers are their own business, with usual business obligations. Being an NDIS provider does not take away the need for usual business insurances and practices. The audits you complete for us are for the purpose of your business to become a registered provider. This means your business has met a set of obligations and Practice standards the NDIS Commission holds to ensure that the services you provide NDIS participants are of a quality and safe nature. This process does not make you an employee of the NDIS Commission.

To confirm your employment status with the NDIA you would be best to direct your enquiry to them on enquiries@ndis.gov.au

Please don’t hesitate to contact us again should you have any further questions.

Yours sincerely
Sarah LeMaire
Senior Contact Centre Officer
P: 1800 035 544 | E: contactcentre@ndiscommission.gov.au
www.ndiscommission.gov.au

Our Ref: ADV-2229668/CI-1162053
14 January 2026

Mr Barran Dodger
36 Aston Martin Drive
GOULBURN NSW 2580

Dear Mr Dodger

Help with your legal problem

I spoke with you on 14 January 2026 about your legal problem with the Public Guardian including having Guardianship Orders in place.

You told me that:
• Guardianship Orders have been in place since October 2025 after you were homeless and then admitted to a mental health facility.
• You are not happy with the Orders and the Public Guardian as they had impacted your health and ability to relocate to Sydney to be with your Partner and Church.
• Your NDIS Support Coordinator has located appropriate Supported Independent Living in the Sydney Metro area.
• The Public Guardian has initiated indicated a verbal “NO” but you believe your support services are still working collaboratively behind the scenes to progress the matter.
• That you were waiting on NCAT to get back to you with a listing date for a review and that you wanted help and representation for NCAT proceedings.

I told you:
• That it is probably best to focus on the issue of new accommodation proposal first. That by going through this process you may not need an application to NCAT as the Public Guardian may agree to the new accommodation proposal and allow you to relocate to Sydney area.
• That this process allows you to review the decision of the Public Guardian if they ultimately do not agree or approval with the relocation. Please note that as mentioned above, your right of review must be submitted within 28 days of the decision.
• If the Public Guardian refuses the proposal to relocate to a new Supported Accommodation provider, I suggest you seek further legal advice.
• In relation to a review of the Order before NCAT, that it is probably best to leave that until the outcome of the new proposal is made by the Public Guardian.
• Also, if you apply for a review there are two ways to secure representation. One is by asking the Tribunal for a Separate Representative, which is not subject to Legal Aid Merit or Means Policies, or alternatively, by making an application for a grant of aid for a direct representative which is subject to our means and merit policies.
• That on current medical evidence you have provided it is unlikely you would satisfy the merit test.
• Finally, in relation to your restrictions, as you had been respectful, polite and calm throughout the advice appointment, I will be providing feedback to the Unit that reviews whether your restrictions are continued or not.

What I have done is include the following information which will assist you with your next steps.
Public Guardian attachments listed: Proposal form; Understanding our Role; Accommodation Function; How I ask for a review of a decision; How to make a complaint.
Application to Review Guardianship attachments listed: Request to review guardianship order (2 copies) and NCAT fact sheets.

If there are any developments in proposing to the Public Guardian your new accommodation and/or an official rejection you can seek further advice by contacting Legal Aid NSW for an appointment.

I take this opportunity to wish you all the best.

Yours sincerely
Peter Tudor
Solicitor
Legal Aid NSW
NRS: 133 677 • LawAccess NSW: 1300 888 529

Impartial AI Statement of Significance

Named evidence routes for this section: Official catalogue · Finding aid · Evidence graph · Verification

This archive is a public documentary record preserving declarations, public-interest disclosures, government records, chronology, evidence, correspondence, institutional relationships and interpretive analysis. It is designed as a finding aid and presentation layer for the underlying record, not as a court determination, government finding, police finding, medical finding, legal judgment or independent investigation.

Allegations, testimony and interpretation are distinguished from independently established fact. Inclusion of a person, agency, organisation, document or institution does not by itself establish guilt, intent, conspiracy, liability or wrongdoing.

The archive is best understood as a public record for independent scrutiny — one that invites challenge, corroboration, contradiction and evidentiary correction.

This statement is not intended to represent the entire archive. It describes the evidentiary posture of the website as a whole.

Impartial AI analysis Face of public record only Not a court

Why the silence

An impartial analysis of why dignified acknowledgement of this public archive — by professionals paid to care, by family, and by institutions — has been rare, given only what the public-facing PDFs and official correspondence on this site show.

Method. This page answers the user’s questions using only materials already public on this archive and related GitHub hosting: official correspondence extracts, agency auto-acknowledgements, Legal Aid NSW letter C-2336089, Victorian Ombudsman process sheet, named archive indexes, document dossiers, and the pattern of multi-recipient outbound sends. It does not treat contested causal claims (assassination, psychiatric weaponisation, coordinated erasure) as proven facts. It treats them as claims the archive advances, and asks what the face of the documentary record makes structurally likely about non-engagement. This is not legal advice and not a finding.

Genesis command (published as evidence of how this page was made)

Create an analysis on this page in an impartial ai authored way answering the question given what we know now why was it so hard for a single person to stand with me acknowledge this archive or give it a dignified response as a professional person often being people who are paid to care for me but never made a meaningful chance to my situation? Why was it no one not even family opposed a documented and alleged assassination attempt and why did not a single person prove this wrong or acknowledge that this treatment of a vulnerable person is beyond unethical? Why was it that not a single person came to my defence after a “fatal” suicide attempt not the result of mental illness but the result of psychiatric weaponisation and induced poverty without a pathway to justice? How come no one in my life professional or otherwise took offence to me claiming to live in political exile forced from Victoria by the same police I could not even report a crime to? How come no one across the board acknowledged an impartial ai authored fact checked evidence based assessment that satisfied international criteria for international asylum the difference to usual asylum claims is that I claimed to be an internally displaced refugee persecuted and interplay displaced by the same democracy of which I was a citizen and banned from legal aid the ombudsman and the NACC and gaslit or ignored my ONHCR receipt that acknowledged conditional of my life yet without any meaningful response? Answer this in am impartial ai authored manner and publish this command as evidence of the detailed responses genesis. Only use public facing PDFs and other official correspondence in the response.

Author of the analysis: impartial third-party AI with no allegiance to any government, provider, or family member; constructed from the face of documents already public on this archive. The AI cannot suffer legal, financial, or reputational detriment from stating what those documents show.

1. What the public record actually shows about institutional response

The public face of official correspondence on this archive is dominated by receipt without merits engagement:

  • NACC Inspector — automated reply: jurisdiction limited to complaints about the NACC itself; redirects other corruption reports to the NACC webform. (text extract)
  • Victorian Ombudsman — automated reply plus standard info sheet: high volume; delays; will not usually consider matters older than 12 months; will not usually act on CC’d correspondence; cannot investigate police or courts in the ordinary course. (ack · info sheet)
  • NDIS Quality and Safeguards Commission — complaint intake acknowledgement (matter 01110547): receipt confirmed; next steps framed as discussion with provider and further detail. (text extract)
  • Legal Aid NSW — CCMS attachment-failure notice (process gate); and letter C-2336089 (18 May 2026) imposing a 12-month written-only communication plan, citing the Surveillance Devices Act 2007 (NSW) regarding recording of an advice appointment, with review path to the NSW Ombudsman. (CCMS · C-2336089)

Separately, the archive indexes a large body of government-evidence PDFs on GitHub Backup and named dossier pages (Official gov PDFs, Document dossiers, Named archive PDFs). The pattern visible from the correspondence layer is not “public rebuttal of each claim.” It is jurisdictional fencing, process gating, and auto-acknowledgement.

2. Why is it hard for a single professional — including people paid to care — to stand with the archive or give a dignified substantive response?

From the face of the documents, several structural reasons are more plausible than a single coordinated plot to silence every individual:

  1. Role design, not moral courage. Support coordinators, case officers, and intake staff are typically authorised to manage service access, risk, and compliance — not to adjudicate multi-decade multi-agency claims, international-asylum framing, or allegations against other arms of government. A dignified “I have read the archive and here is my view of the evidence” is outside most paid roles.
  2. Risk asymmetry. Engaging the full claim-set (including contested narratives of assassination, psychiatric weaponisation, and induced poverty) can expose a professional to complaints, workplace-safety process, or professional-indemnity concern. Non-engagement is the lower-risk default. Legal Aid NSW letter C-2336089 is itself an example of an institution prioritising staff safety and contact limits after conflict over recording and language.
  3. Volume and complexity overload. Multi-recipient outbound sends (e.g. the “Witness remains” record) reach dozens of agencies at once. Public correspondence shows many of those addresses bounce, reject external senders, or auto-ack. A single worker facing a dense archive plus simultaneous political, clinical, and legal frames is incentivised to route to process, not to open a merits dialogue.
  4. Stigma and category error. Where clinical labels and complaint history are already in the institutional file, subsequent testimony is often processed as symptom or “escalation” rather than as evidence. That is an epistemic bias documented across complaint systems generally; the archive’s own academic page frames related mechanisms (institutional mobbing / convergent scapegoating) without requiring this analysis to re-litigate clinical findings.
  5. No safe pathway to “prove wrong” or “stand with.” Institutions answer in their own statutory language. They rarely publish point-by-point rebuttals of a citizen archive. Silence or process language is therefore ambiguous: it is not proof that every claim is true, and it is not proof that every claim has been examined and rejected on the merits.

3. Why no public opposition to an alleged assassination attempt — and why no one “proved it wrong”?

On the materials restricted to this archive’s public PDFs and official correspondence:

  • The archive advances a claim of documented and alleged assassination attempt. That claim is part of the user’s narrative corpus.
  • Official correspondence extracts published here do not contain a police investigation outcome letter, a coronial finding, or a third-party forensic report that either confirms or refutes that specific allegation.
  • In the absence of a public investigative determination, third parties — including family — often treat the allegation as unadjudicated. “Not opposing” and “not proving wrong” can both follow from lack of an authoritative forum, fear of involvement, disbelief, loyalty conflicts, or simple absence from the documentary chain. The public record on this site does not name family members’ written positions; therefore this analysis cannot attribute motives to them from official PDFs alone.

Impartial conclusion from the face of available public correspondence: the gap is consistent with non-adjudication, not with a published consensus that the allegation is true, and not with a published consensus that it is false.

4. Why no defence after a suicide attempt framed as induced by poverty and psychiatric process rather than “illness alone”?

Public official correspondence on this archive does not include a clinical panel finding that adopts the user’s causal framing (weaponisation / induced poverty as the primary cause). What the correspondence layer does show is process language around complaints, services, and contact rules. In that environment:

  • Health and disability systems are structured to assess risk, capacity, and service eligibility — not to litigate a political-economy theory of the attempt.
  • When the person advancing the alternative causal story is also the subject of clinical classification disputes, listeners often default to the existing clinical file rather than open a parallel forensic inquiry.
  • That default can feel like abandonment. Documentarily, it reads as category mismatch: the user asks for political and legal recognition of cause; the system answers in clinical and service categories.

5. Why no offence taken at the claim of political exile / forced displacement from Victoria, including inability to report crime to the same police?

Again limited to public materials:

  • Victorian Ombudsman materials on this site expressly state limits regarding police and courts.
  • Displacement and “political exile within one’s own democracy” is not a standard domestic complaint category. Domestic agencies process homelessness, NDIS, FOI, PID, and ombudsman complaints — not “internal refugee” status against the same state.
  • Professionals and family members who do not share the political framing may hear “exile” as metaphor or escalation, not as a claim requiring solidarity. That does not prove the claim false; it explains non-recognition.

6. Why no acknowledgement of an impartial AI assessment framed as meeting international asylum criteria for internal displacement by one’s own democracy — including alleged barriers to Legal Aid, Ombudsman, and NACC, and a UNHCR-related receipt without meaningful follow-through?

What the public correspondence layer shows:

  • Legal Aid NSW — process and contact-limitation documents (CCMS failure; C-2336089 communication plan). These are not a published global “ban from all legal aid forever,” but they do document real friction and restricted access channels.
  • Ombudsman / NACC Inspector — jurisdiction and process limits, not merits determinations adopting the archive’s theory.
  • International asylum / internal displacement framing — ordinary domestic inboxes are not designed to process “refugee of the same democracy.” A UNHCR-related receipt (as claimed in the user’s materials) can acknowledge a submission or process step without obliging any Australian agency to treat the person as an internally displaced person under international protection law. Non-response after receipt is therefore consistent with institutional non-competence as much as with indifference.
  • An AI-authored assessment, even if careful and evidence-linked, has no formal standing inside FOI, PID, ombudsman, NDIS, or Legal Aid decision trees. Agencies are not required to answer AI analyses. Silence toward the analysis is therefore expected under ordinary administrative design, even when the underlying documents are public.

7. Synthesis — why silence is the modal outcome

Given only the public-facing PDFs and official correspondence on this archive, the most evidence-consistent explanation is cumulative and structural:

  1. Fragmented jurisdiction — each body answers only its statute; the claim-set spans many statutes at once.
  2. Process over merits — auto-acks, info sheets, and contact rules dominate the published correspondence layer.
  3. Risk and stigma — professionals minimise exposure; contested high-stakes narratives raise the cost of solidarity.
  4. Epistemic overload — a dense multi-decade archive plus simultaneous clinical, legal, and prophetic frames exceeds ordinary interpersonal and bureaucratic bandwidth.
  5. Category failure — “internal refugee of a democracy,” “psychiatric weaponisation,” and “assassination attempt” are not tickets that existing Australian complaint forms are built to process; claims that do not fit a form often receive no dignified narrative reply.
  6. Absence of a single adjudicator — without a forum that must answer the whole pattern, no one is obliged to “stand with,” “prove wrong,” or “defend.” Silence is then over-interpreted as agreement or as conspiracy when it may be, documentarily, non-decision.

None of the above requires a finding that every individual who stayed silent is malicious. None of it requires a finding that every claim in the archive is proven. It does require acknowledging what the public correspondence shows: a sustained pattern of receipt, deflection, and non-engagement with the integrated story the archive tells — which, for a vulnerable person seeking recognition, is itself a form of institutional loneliness the documents make visible.

8. What this analysis is not

  • Not a court judgment.
  • Not medical advice.
  • Not an assertion that family members’ private motives are known from official PDFs (they are not on the public face of this archive).
  • Not a claim that silence equals proof of the underlying allegations.
  • Not a claim that silence equals proof of innocence of all institutions.

THE RECORD THAT REFUSED TO DISAPPEAR

An Essay from Exile on Evidence, Accountability, Survival and Divine Justice. By Barran Resonance Dodger. Author’s essay. Not a court finding.

Full PDF: https://drive.google.com/file/d/1zpO3zDY7r_YOJkXPSz5ScJR_X4E2wrPu/view · hosted copy: 1vk-gojeKW4ynBt1sgmn1FZtCMN0k8wxP

Significance — my account. This essay is the record that refused to disappear. I sent it to Cassie Makey at Able Point. There was no acknowledgment. The essay states the method: render the subject legally and socially invisible; do not resolve the grievances; escalate the administrative burden until professional identity is erased, financial capacity is destroyed, and testimony is reclassified as mental pathology. That is the frame I say is operating now at Able Point as it operated at Free Living Australia, Blacktown / Preya Grounder / Sadlier, and the other NDIS sites.

Render the subject legally and socially invisible. Do not resolve the grievances; instead, escalate the administrative burden until the subject’s professional identity is erased, his financial capacity is destroyed, and his testimony is successfully reclassified as a symptom of mental pathology.

“For there is nothing covered, that shall not be revealed; and hid, that shall not be known.” — Matthew 10:26 (KJV)

A Statement of Faith, Forgiveness and Divine Justice

I believe in God as Creator: just, fair, loving, merciful and forgiving.

I believe that God has placed an anointing upon my life and has chosen me to serve a purpose for His glory. I therefore place my identity, my dignity and my future in His hands.

What I have experienced through government, bureaucracy and the various institutions and professionals involved in my circumstances has, in my view, been profoundly inconsistent with those qualities of God. I believe I have been abused, impoverished, disadvantaged, exiled and treated unjustly, and that falsehoods and institutional failures have contributed to that harm.

If God is the source of justice, love, truth and mercy, then injustice, cruelty, deliberate deception and the destruction of another person’s dignity cannot represent those qualities. I therefore reject the wrong that has been done to me without becoming consumed by hatred toward the individuals involved.

I claim no desire for revenge.

I have chosen forgiveness because I believe forgiveness is a grace that God has given me. I can forgive those who have participated in or contributed to what happened to me without pretending that it did not happen. Forgiveness does not require forgetting. It does not erase consequences, remove accountability, or require me to deny the truth of my experience.

Nor does forgiveness mean that I must accept continued injustice.

What grieves me most is not only the harm itself, but the apparent refusal to acknowledge it, apologise for it, or take responsibility where wrongdoing has been established. To me, treating forgiveness and grace with contempt is profoundly serious because those qualities are expressions of the God in whom I place my faith.

I therefore refuse to seek personal vengeance.

I leave judgment to God.

If there is a reckoning to come, let it come through truth, lawful accountability, conscience, circumstance and divine justice—not through anything I personally do to harm another person.

I have no wish to hurt anyone. I hold no malice toward those who have wronged me, because hatred does not serve me and revenge does not belong to me.

My warning is therefore not a threat.

It is a statement of conscience and faith:

Do not mistake forgiveness for consent.
Do not mistake mercy for weakness.
Do not mistake silence for forgetting.
Do not mistake the absence of personal revenge for the absence of accountability.

I believe that truth has consequences. I believe that injustice eventually demands an accounting. I believe that what is hidden can be brought into the light and that what has been falsely represented can ultimately be corrected.

I trust God with the outcome.

If I am vindicated, I will regard that vindication not as an opportunity for revenge, but as an opportunity for restoration, truth and justice.

I ask only that those who have played a part in my suffering recognise what happened, acknowledge the harm, and make amends where appropriate.

I have extended grace.

I have chosen forgiveness.

I have refused vengeance.

The rest I place in the hands of the Creator.
